|\gamma_{0}(y)-\gamma_{0}(y^{\prime})|\leq|\widetilde{\gamma}_{0}(y)-\widetilde{\gamma}_{0}(y^{\prime})|
, and Item
2
in
2.11
implies that, for any
t\geq 0
,

(2.73)

\displaystyle\gamma_{t}(0)-\gamma_{t}(A)\leq\widetilde{\gamma}_{t}(0)-\widetilde{\gamma}_{t}(A).

Next we use
Item

1
in
2.11
, by comparing to delta masses at
0
and
-x/{\mathfrak{C}}
, respectively, to show that
\widetilde{\gamma}_{t}(0)\leq 2\sqrt{tA}
and
\widetilde{\gamma}_{t}(A)\geq-x/{\mathfrak{C}}-2\sqrt{tA}
. The proofs of the two statements are very similar, so we will only prove the first one. Denote
\widehat{\mu}_{0}:=A\delta_{0}
, and let
\widehat{\mu}_{t}=\widehat{\mu}_{0}\boxplus\mu_{\semci}^{(t)}
be the free convolution of
\widehat{\mu}_{0}
with the rescaled semicircle distribution
\mu_{\semci}^{(t)}
. Then recall from
Example

2.5
that
\widehat{\mu}_{t}
is given explicitly by

\displaystyle{\rm d}\widehat{\mu}_{t}=\frac{\sqrt{4At-x^{2}}}{2\pi t}{\rm d}x.

For the measure valued process
\{\widehat{\mu}_{t}\}_{0\leq t\leq{\mathsf{T}}}
, we denote the associated inverted cumulative density function by

\widehat{\gamma}_{t}:[0,A]\mapsto{\mathbb{R}}
. From construction, for
y\in[0,A]
we have
\widetilde{\gamma}_{0}(y)\leq 0=\widehat{\gamma}_{0}(y)
. Thus Item
1
in
2.11
implies that for any
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\gamma}_{t}(0)\leq\widehat{\gamma}_{t}(0)=2\sqrt{At}.

The same argument also gives that
\widetilde{\gamma}_{t}(A)\geq-x/{\mathfrak{C}}-2\sqrt{At}
. Together with (
2.73
), we conclude that for
t\leq\sqrt{\mathfrak{C}x}
we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{t}(0)-\gamma_{t}(A)\leq\widetilde{\gamma}_{t}(0)-\widetilde{\gamma}_{t}(A)\leq x/{\mathfrak{C}}+4\sqrt{At}\leq x\left(\frac{1}{{\mathfrak{C}}}+4\sqrt{\frac{{\mathsf{b}}}{{\mathfrak{C}}}}\right)\leq x,

where in the third inequality we used
A={\mathsf{b}}(x/{\mathfrak{C}})^{3/2}
and
t\leq\sqrt{{\mathfrak{C}}x}
; in the last inequality we used
{\mathfrak{C}}=2^{21}/{\mathsf{b}}^{2}\geq\max\{2,2^{6}{\mathsf{b}}\}
. Hence, for
t\leq\sqrt{{\mathfrak{C}}x}
, we have

(2.74)

\displaystyle\mu_{t}([E_{t}-x,E_{t}])\geq A={\mathsf{b}}(x/{\mathfrak{C}})^{3/2}.

For
t\geq\sqrt{{\mathfrak{C}}x}
, either
t\geq{\mathfrak{C}}\sqrt{\eta_{*}}
or
x\geq{\mathfrak{C}}\eta_{*}
. In both cases we have
t\geq{\mathfrak{C}}\sqrt{\eta_{*}}\geq(30/{\mathsf{b}})\sqrt{\eta_{*}}
. Recall
\xi(t)
,
{\mathcal{A}}(t)
,
\mathcal{E}(t)
, and
{\mathfrak{c}}(t)
from
2.14
; for
(30/{\mathsf{b}})\sqrt{\eta_{*}}\leq t\leq{\mathsf{T}}
, we have that

(2.75)

\displaystyle\varrho_{t}(y)=(1+{\mathcal{E}}(E_{t}-y))\frac{\sqrt{{\mathcal{A}}(t)(E_{t}-y)}}{\pi}\geq\frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{{\mathsf{b}}}{2^{5}}\frac{\sqrt{E_{t}-y}}{\pi},

provided that
|y|\leq 2^{-21}{\mathsf{b}}^{2}t^{2}\leq{\mathfrak{c}}(t)/2
.
Moreover, for
t\geq\sqrt{{\mathfrak{C}}x}
, we have
x\leq t^{2}/{\mathfrak{C}}\leq 2^{-21}{\mathsf{b}}^{2}t^{2}\leq{\mathfrak{c}}(t)/2
, where we used that
{\mathfrak{C}}\geq 2^{21}/{\mathsf{b}}^{2}
. Then integrating (
2.75
) gives

(2.76)

\displaystyle\mu_{t}([E_{t}-x,E_{t}])

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{x}\varrho_{t}(E_{t}-y){\rm d}y\geq\int_{0}^{x}\frac{{\mathsf{b}}}{2^{6}\pi}\sqrt{y}{\rm d}y=\frac{{\mathsf{b}}}{96\pi}x^{3/2}.

The estimates (
2.74
) and (
2.76
) together give the first statement in
2.15
.

Next we prove (
2.54
). Recall our assumption that we have either
t\geq{\mathfrak{C}}\sqrt{\eta}_{*}
, or
\kappa\geq{\mathfrak{C}}\eta_{*}
, implying that

\displaystyle{\rm Im}[m_{t}(E_{t}+\kappa+\mathrm{i}\eta)]

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\frac{\eta\varrho_{t}(x){\rm d}x}{(E_{t}+\kappa-x)^{2}+\eta^{2}}\geq\int_{E_{t}-(\kappa+\eta)}^{E_{t}}\frac{\eta\varrho_{t}(x){\rm d}x}{(E_{t}+\kappa-x)^{2}+\eta^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\int_{E_{t}-(\kappa+\eta)}^{E_{t}}\frac{\eta\varrho_{t}(x){\rm d}x}{(2\kappa+\eta)^{2}+\eta^{2}}\geq\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}\eta(\kappa+\eta)^{3/2}}{2(2\kappa+2\eta)^{2}}=\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}\eta}{8\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}},

where in the second statement we restricted the integration on
[E_{t}-\kappa-\eta,E_{t}]
; in the third statement we used that for
x\in[E_{t}-\kappa-\eta,E_{t}]
, we have
E_{t}+\kappa-x\leq 2\kappa+\eta
; in the fourth statement inequality we used
\kappa+\eta\leq{\mathsf{T}}^{2}
from our assumption, and
\mu_{t}([E_{t}-(\kappa+\eta),E_{t}])\geq{\mathfrak{c}}(\kappa+\eta)^{3/2}
from the second statement in
2.15
.
∎

3.
Edge Rigidity and Universality

In this section we study Dyson Brownian motion, with initial data
\mu_{0}=\mu_{0}^{(n)}=(1/n)\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{\lambda_{i}(0)}
satisfying
1.2
, and prove
1.3
and
1.4
. Recall
\mu_{t}=\mu_{0}\boxplus\mu_{\rm sc}^{(t)}
,
E_{t}=\max(\supp\mu_{t})
, and the constants
{\mathfrak{c}}
and
{\mathfrak{C}}
from
2.15
.
We will first study the case that

(3.1)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{C}}^{2}\eta_{*}\geq(\log n)^{15}/n^{2/3}.

Under the assumption (
3.1
), define the function

(3.2)

\displaystyle f(t)=\max\{{\mathfrak{C}}\sqrt{\eta_{*}}-{\mathfrak{c}}\max\{0,t-{\mathfrak{C}}\sqrt{\eta_{*}}\}/8,(\log n)^{15/2}n^{-1/3}\}^{2}.

Then for
t\leq{\mathfrak{C}}\sqrt{\eta_{*}}
, we have by (
3.1
) that
f(t)={\mathfrak{C}}^{2}\eta_{*}
; for
t\geq{\mathfrak{C}}\sqrt{\eta}_{*}
,
f(t)
decreases to
(\log n)^{15}/n^{2/3}
, and

(3.3)

\displaystyle(f(s))^{1/2}-(f(t))^{1/2}\leq\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}(t-s)}{8},\quad{\mathfrak{C}}\sqrt{\eta_{*}}\leq s\leq t.

The following proposition states that
\lambda_{1}(t)\leq E_{t}+f(t)
for
0\leq t\leq{\mathsf{T}}
with overwhelming probability.
1.3
is an easy consequence of it. The proof of
3.1
will be given in
Section

3.2
.

Proposition 3.1
.

Consider Dyson Brownian motion
\bm{\lambda}(t)
as in (
1.2
). We assume its initial data
\mu_{0}=\mu_{0}^{(n)}=(1/n)\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{\lambda_{i}(0)}
satisfies
1.2
. We further assume that (
3.1
) holds and
{\mathsf{T}}\geq{\mathfrak{B}}\sqrt{\eta_{*}}=2^{60}{\mathsf{b}}^{-6}\sqrt{\eta_{*}}
. Recall
\mu_{t}=\mu_{0}\boxplus\mu_{\rm sc}^{(t)}
and
E_{t}=\max(\supp\mu_{t})
from
2.15
. There exists a large constant
C=C({\mathsf{b}},{\mathsf{T}})>1
, such that, with probability
1-Ce^{-(\log n)^{2}}
, we have for any
0\leq t\leq{\mathsf{T}}
that

(3.4)

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}(t)\leq E_{t}+f(t),

where
f(t)
is as defined in (
3.2
).

Proof of
1.3
.

Recall
{\mathfrak{c}}
and
{\mathfrak{C}}
from
2.15
. We first discuss the case when (
3.1
) holds.
From the construction of
f(t)
in (
3.2
) and discussion after it, for
t\geq 2^{60}{\mathsf{b}}^{-6}\sqrt{\eta_{*}}\geq(1+8/{\mathfrak{c}}){\mathfrak{C}}\sqrt{\eta_{*}}
,
we have
f(t)=\max\{0,(\log n)^{15/2}n^{-1/3}\}^{2}=(\log n)^{15}n^{-2/3}
. The claim (
1.8
) thus follows from (
3.4
). When (
3.1
) does not hold, then
1.2
still holds by replacing
\eta_{*}
with
(\log n)^{15}/({\mathfrak{C}}^{2}n^{2/3})
. If
\eta_{*}=(\log n)^{15}/({\mathfrak{C}}^{2}n^{2/3})
, we can construct
f(t)
as in (
3.2
), then
f(t)=(\log n)^{15}n^{-2/3}
for any
t\geq 0
. Thus
3.1
gives that with probability
1-Ce^{-(\log n)^{2}}
,
\lambda_{1}(t)\leq E_{t}+(\log n)^{15}/n^{2/3}
for any
0\leq t\leq{\mathsf{T}}
, and the claim (
1.8
) follows.
∎

3.1.
Characteristic flow

In the remainder of
Section

3
, we will use the notations
{\mathsf{T}},{\mathsf{b}},\eta_{*}
and the measure
\mu_{0}=\mu_{0}^{(n)}
from
1.2
.
We recall the functions
{\mathcal{A}}(t),{\mathfrak{c}}(t)
, and constants
{\mathfrak{c}}
and
{\mathfrak{C}}
from
2.15
. We also recall the functions
f(t)
from (
3.2
). We denote the free convolution of
\mu_{0}
with rescaled semicircle distribution by as
\mu_{t}
(recall from
Section

2.1
). We let
E_{t}=\max(\supp\mu_{t})
and denote the Stieltjes transform of
\mu_{t}
by
m_{t}(z)
. We recall the set
\Lambda_{t}
from (
2.7
).

We can reformulate the defining relation (
2.8
) of the free convolution by introducing the characteristic flow:

(3.5)

\displaystyle m_{t}(z_{t}(u))=m_{0}(u),\quad z_{t}(u)=u-tm_{0}(u)=:E_{t}+\kappa_{t}(u)+\mathrm{i}\eta_{t}(u),\quad u\in\Lambda_{t}.

When the context is clear, we will simply write
z_{t}(u),\kappa_{t}(u),\eta_{t}(u)
as
z_{t},\kappa_{t},\eta_{t}
.
It follows from (
3.5
) that
z_{t}=u-tm_{t}(z_{t})\in\mathbb{H}
, since
z_{t}:\Lambda_{t}\rightarrow\mathbb{H}
is a bijection, by
2.2
. The characteristic flow satisfies the fundamental property that

(3.6)

\displaystyle m_{t}(z_{t})\qquad\text{is constant in $t\geq 0$},

which we will often use without comment.

In the following we collect some basic facts about the Stieltjes transform and the characteristic flows, which will be used repeatedly in the rest of this section. Take any point
u\in\mathbb{H}
, and time
t\geq 0
, such that at time
t
we have
z_{t}=z_{t}(u)=E_{t}+\kappa_{t}+\mathrm{i}\eta_{t}\in\Lambda_{t}
. Then for any time
0\leq s\leq t
, using
z_{s}=u-sm_{0}(u)=u-sm_{t}(z_{t})
(from (
3.5
)) we have

(3.7)

\displaystyle z_{s}=z_{t}+(t-s)m_{t}(z_{t}),\quad\eta_{s}=\eta_{t}+(t-s){\rm Im}[m_{t}(z_{t})]\geq\eta_{t},

where the last inequality follows from
{\rm Im}[m_{t}(z_{t})]={\rm Im}[m_{0}(u)]\geq 0
.

We collect some further properties of the characteristic flow in the following two lemmas.

3.2
states that if
\kappa_{t}\geq 0
, then
\kappa_{s}\geq 0
for all
0\leq s\leq t
; it also gives lower bounds on
\kappa_{s}-\kappa_{t}
.
3.3
states that if
\kappa_{t}\geq f(t)
(recall from (
3.2
)), then
\kappa_{s}\geq f(s)
for all
0\leq s\leq t
; it also gives lower bounds on
\kappa_{s}-f(s)
.

Lemma 3.2
.

Adopt
1.2
, and recall the constants
{\mathfrak{c}}
and
{\mathfrak{C}}
from
2.15
. Take
u\in\Lambda_{t}
, and fix real numbers
t>0
and
0\leq s\leq t
. Denote
z_{s}=z_{s}(u)=E_{s}+\kappa_{s}+\mathrm{i}\eta_{s}
, and assume that
\kappa_{s}\leq{\mathsf{T}}^{2}
(recall
{\mathsf{T}}
from
1.2
). If
\kappa_{t}\geq 0
then, for
0\leq s\leq t
, we have

(3.8)

\displaystyle\kappa_{s}-\kappa_{t}\geq\left(\frac{\eta_{s}}{\eta_{t}}-1\right)\kappa_{t}=(t-s){\rm Im}[m_{t}(z_{t})]\frac{\kappa_{t}}{\eta_{t}}\geq 0.

Moreover, if
\kappa_{t}\geq 0
then, for
{\mathfrak{C}}\sqrt{\eta_{*}}\leq s\leq t
, we have

(3.9)

\displaystyle\kappa_{s}^{1/2}-\kappa_{t}^{1/2}\geq\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}(t-s)}{4}.

Lemma 3.3
.

Adopt
1.2
, and recall the constants
{\mathfrak{c}}
and
{\mathfrak{C}}
from
2.15
. Take
u\in\Lambda_{t}
, and fix real numbers
t>0
and
0\leq s\leq t
. Denote
z_{s}=z_{s}(u)=E_{s}+\kappa_{s}+\mathrm{i}\eta_{s}
, and assume that
\kappa_{s}\leq{\mathsf{T}}^{2}
(recall
{\mathsf{T}}
from
1.2
). If
\kappa_{t}\geq f(t)
(recall from (
3.2
)) then, for
{\mathfrak{C}}\sqrt{\eta_{*}}\leq s\leq t
, we have

(3.10)

\displaystyle\kappa_{s}-f(s)\geq\frac{(t-s)}{2}{\rm Im}[m_{t}(z_{t})]\frac{\kappa_{t}}{\eta_{t}};

Moreover, if
\kappa_{t}\geq f(t)
then, for
0\leq s\leq\widetilde{t}:=\min\{t,{\mathfrak{C}}\sqrt{\eta_{*}}\}
, we have

(3.11)

\displaystyle\kappa_{s}-f(s)\geq(\widetilde{t}-s){\rm Im}[m_{t}(z_{t})]\frac{\kappa_{\widetilde{t}}}{\eta_{\widetilde{t}}}

Proof of
3.2
.

We will first show that, if
\kappa_{t}\geq 0
, then
\kappa_{s}\geq 0
for
0\leq s\leq t
, and we will also estimate its derivative. Notice

(3.12)

\displaystyle\del_{s}{\rm Re}[z_{s}]=\del_{s}{\rm Re}[u-sm_{0}(u)]=-{\rm Re}[m_{0}(u)]=-{\rm Re}[m_{s}(z_{s})],

where the first statement is from the definition (
3.5
) that
z_{s}=u-sm_{0}(u)
;
the second statement is from taking derivative with respect to
s
;
the last statement follows from (
3.6
). Further recall the construction from Item
5
in
2.3
that there exists
w_{s}\in{\mathbb{R}}
such that
m_{0}^{\prime}(w_{s})=1/s
and
E_{s}=w_{s}-sm_{0}(w_{s})=z_{s}(w_{s})
. By taking the time derivative on both sides, we get a similar equation as (
3.12
), namely,

(3.13)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\del_{s}E_{s}&=\del_{s}w_{s}-sm_{0}^{\prime}(w_{s})\del_{s}w_{s}-m_{0}(w_{s})\\
&=\del_{s}w_{s}(1-sm_{0}^{\prime}(w_{s}))-m_{0}(w_{s})=-m_{0}(w_{s})=-m_{s}(E_{s}),\end{split}

where the first statement follows from taking the derivative with respect to
s
on both sides of
E_{s}=w_{s}-sm_{0}(w_{s})
;
the second and third statements use
m_{0}^{\prime}(w_{s})=1/s
; and the last statement uses (
3.6
) and
E_{s}=z_{s}(w_{s})
.
Then by taking difference between (
3.12
) and (
3.13
), we get

(3.14)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\del_{s}\kappa_{s}&=-({\rm Re}[m_{s}(z_{s})]-m_{s}(E_{s}))=-(({\rm Re}[m_{s}(z_{s})]-m_{s}({\rm Re}[z_{s}])+(m_{s}({\rm Re}[z_{s}])-m_{s}(E_{s})))\\
&=-\eta_{s}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\varrho_{s}(x){\rm d}x}{((E_{s}+\kappa_{s}-x)^{2}+\eta_{s}^{2})(E_{s}+\kappa_{s}-x)}-\kappa_{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\varrho_{s}(x){\rm d}x}{(E_{s}-x)(E_{s}+\kappa_{s}-x)},\end{split}

where the first statement follows from
\kappa_{s}={\rm Re}[z_{s}]-E_{s}
; the second statement is from separating the difference into two; in the third statement we used
{\rm Re}[z_{s}]=E_{s}+\kappa_{s}
and
{\rm Re}[m_{s}(z_{s})]=\int_{\mathbb{R}}(x-E_{s}-\kappa_{s})\varrho_{s}(x){\rm d}x/((E_{s}+\kappa_{s}-x)^{2}+\eta_{s}^{2})
.
By our assumption, we have
\kappa_{t}\geq 0
. Next we will show that
\kappa_{s}\geq 0
for all
0\leq s\leq t
. Otherwise, there exists
\tau=\sup\{0\leq s\leq t:\kappa_{s}<0\}
. Since
\kappa_{s}
is continuous, we have that
\tau\in[0,t)
,
\kappa_{s}\geq 0
for
s\in[\tau,t]
and
\kappa_{\tau}=0
. Thus for
s\in[\tau,t]
, (
3.14
) gives that

(3.15)

\displaystyle\del_{s}\kappa_{s}\leq 0,

since
E_{s}+\kappa_{s}\geq E_{s}=\max\supp\varrho_{s}
, and
\kappa_{s}\geq 0
. By integrating (
3.15
) from
\tau
to
t
, we conclude that
\kappa_{\tau}\geq\kappa_{t}\geq 0
, which contradicts with our choice of
\tau
. Thus we conclude

(3.16)

\displaystyle\kappa_{s}\geq 0\quad\text{and $\kappa_{s}$ is decreasing for}\quad 0\leq s\leq t,

where the second statement follows from
\del_{s}\kappa_{s}<0
(from (
3.14
)).

We can also lower bound the second integral in (
3.14
) by

(3.17)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\varrho_{s}(x){\rm d}x}{(E_{s}-x)(E_{s}+\kappa_{s}-x)}\geq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\varrho_{s}(x){\rm d}x}{|z_{s}-x|^{2}}=\frac{1}{\eta_{s}}{\rm Im}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\varrho_{s}(x){\rm d}x}{x-z_{s}}=\frac{{\rm Im}[m_{s}(z_{s})]}{\eta_{s}},

where in the first statement we used
z_{s}=E_{s}+\kappa_{s}+\mathrm{i}\eta_{s}
with
\kappa_{s}\geq 0
from (
3.16
) and
\max\supp\varrho_{s}=E_{s}
, thus
|z_{s}-x|\geq E_{s}+\kappa_{s}-x\geq E_{s}-x
; the last two statements are from the definition of Stieltjes transform (
2.1
). Combining (
3.14
) and (
3.17
), we get

(3.18)

\displaystyle\del_{s}\kappa_{s}\leq-\frac{\kappa_{s}}{\eta_{s}}{\rm Im}[m_{s}(z_{s})].

Recall from the definition of the characteristic flow (
3.5
) and (
3.7
) that the derivative for the imaginary part
\del_{s}\eta_{s}
satisfies

\del_{s}\eta_{s}=-{\rm Im}[m_{s}(z_{s})].

By plugging this relation to (
3.18
), we conclude

\displaystyle\del_{s}\kappa_{s}\leq\frac{\kappa_{s}}{\eta_{s}}\del_{s}\eta_{s},\qquad\text{so that}\qquad\frac{\del_{s}\eta_{s}}{\eta_{s}}\geq\frac{\del_{s}\kappa_{s}}{\kappa_{s}},

and hence for
0\leq s\leq t
we obtain

(3.19)

\displaystyle\kappa_{s}\geq\frac{\eta_{s}}{\eta_{t}}\kappa_{t}.

Recall from (
3.7
) that
(\eta_{s}-\eta_{t})/\eta_{t}=(t-s){\rm Im}[m_{t}(z_{t})]/\eta_{t}
. Hence, the claim (
3.8
) follows from (
3.19
):

(3.20)

\displaystyle\kappa_{s}-\kappa_{t}\geq\left(\frac{\eta_{s}}{\eta_{t}}-1\right)\kappa_{t}=(t-s){\rm Im}[m_{t}(z_{t})]\frac{\kappa_{t}}{\eta_{t}}.

If we moreover have that
s\geq{\mathfrak{C}}\sqrt{\eta_{*}}
, then we can also bound the integral in (
3.14
) by

(3.21)

\displaystyle\begin{split}-\kappa_{s}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\varrho_{s}(x){\rm d}x}{(E_{s}-x)(E_{s}+\kappa_{s}-x)}\leq-\kappa_{s}\int_{E_{s}-\kappa_{s}}^{E_{s}}\frac{\varrho_{s}(x){\rm d}x}{2\kappa_{s}^{2}}=-\frac{\mu([E_{s}-\kappa_{s},E_{s}])}{2\kappa_{s}}\leq-\frac{1}{2}{\mathfrak{c}}\kappa_{s}^{1/2},\end{split}

where we in the first inequality we restricted the integral on
[E_{s}-\kappa_{s},E_{s}]
and used
\kappa_{s}\geq E_{s}-x
and
2\kappa_{s}\geq E_{s}+\kappa_{s}-x
on this interval; in the last two inequalities, we used
\kappa_{s}\leq{\mathsf{T}}^{2}
and
\mu_{s}([E_{s}-\kappa_{s},E_{s}])\geq{\mathfrak{c}}\kappa_{s}^{3/2}
from
2.15
. Plugging (
3.21
) into (
3.14
) we get

(3.22)

\displaystyle\del_{s}\kappa_{s}\leq-\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}}{2}\kappa_{s}^{1/2},\quad{\mathfrak{C}}\sqrt{\eta_{*}}\leq s\leq t.

Solving it we obtain

(3.23)

\displaystyle\kappa_{s}^{1/2}-\kappa_{t}^{1/2}\geq\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}(t-s)}{4},\quad{\mathfrak{C}}\sqrt{\eta_{*}}\leq s\leq t.

This finishes the proof of (
3.9
)
∎

Proof of
3.3
.

For
t\geq s\geq{\mathfrak{C}}\sqrt{\eta_{*}}
, the estimates (
3.8
) and (
3.9
) imply

(3.24)

\displaystyle\kappa_{s}-\kappa_{t}\geq(t-s){\rm Im}[m_{t}(z_{t})]\frac{\kappa_{t}}{\eta_{t}},\quad\kappa_{s}-\kappa_{t}\geq\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}(t-s)}{4}(\sqrt{\kappa_{s}}+\sqrt{\kappa_{t}}),

From our construction of
f(t)
, using (
3.3
) and (
3.9
), and recalling that
\kappa_{t}\geq f(t)
, we have

(3.25)

\displaystyle\sqrt{f(s)}\leq\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}(t-s)}{8}+\sqrt{f(t)}\leq\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}(t-s)}{8}+\sqrt{\kappa_{t}}\leq\sqrt{\kappa_{s}},\quad{\mathfrak{C}}\sqrt{\eta_{*}}\leq s\leq t,

Rearranging (
3.3
), together with (
3.25
), gives
\kappa_{s}\geq f(s)\geq f(t)
. Thus,

(3.26)

\displaystyle f(s)-f(t)\leq\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}}{8}(t-s)(\sqrt{f(s)}+\sqrt{f(t)})\leq\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}(t-s)}{8}(\sqrt{\kappa_{s}}+\sqrt{\kappa_{t}}),\quad{\mathfrak{C}}\sqrt{\eta_{*}}\leq s\leq t,

Put everything together, we get

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\kappa_{s}-f(s)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}(\kappa_{s}-\kappa_{t})+\frac{1}{2}(\kappa_{s}-\kappa_{t})-(f(s)-f(t))

\displaystyle\geq\frac{(t-s)}{2}{\rm Im}[m_{t}(z_{t})]\frac{\kappa_{t}}{\eta_{t}}+\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}(t-s)}{8}(\sqrt{\kappa_{s}}+\sqrt{\kappa_{t}})-\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}(t-s)}{8}(\sqrt{\kappa_{s}}+\sqrt{\kappa_{t}})

\displaystyle=\frac{(t-s)}{2}{\rm Im}[m_{t}(z_{t})]\frac{\kappa_{t}}{\eta_{t}},

where in the first inequality we used our assumption
\kappa_{t}\geq f(t)
in
3.3
; in the second inequality we used (
3.24
) to bound the first two terms and (
3.26
) to bound the last term.
This gives (
3.10
).

Next we prove (
3.11
), by considering two cases.
If
t=\widetilde{t}
, then
f(\widetilde{t})=f(t)\leq\kappa_{t}=\kappa_{\widetilde{t}}
. If
t>\widetilde{t}
, then
\widetilde{t}={\mathfrak{C}}\sqrt{\eta_{*}}
and (
3.10
) (applied with the
s
there equal to
\widetilde{t}
here) implies
f(\widetilde{t})\leq\kappa_{\widetilde{t}}
. In both cases we have
f(\widetilde{t})\leq\kappa_{\widetilde{t}}
.
For
s\leq\widetilde{t}
we have
f(s)=f(\widetilde{t})\leq\kappa_{\widetilde{t}}
, and (
3.8
) (applied with the
t
there equal to
\widetilde{t}
here, observing that
\kappa_{\tilde{t}}\geq\kappa_{t}\geq f(t)\geq 0
holds) implies

(3.27)

\displaystyle\kappa_{s}-f(s)\geq\kappa_{s}-\kappa_{\widetilde{t}}\geq({\widetilde{t}}-s){\rm Im}[m_{s}(z_{s})]\frac{\kappa_{\widetilde{t}}}{\eta_{\widetilde{s}}}=({\widetilde{t}}-s){\rm Im}[m_{t}(z_{t})]\frac{\kappa_{\widetilde{t}}}{\eta_{\widetilde{t}}}.

This finishes the proof of (
3.11
).
∎

Figure 3.
The spectral domain
{\mathcal{D}}_{t}
constructed in (
3.28
).

Fix the real number
{\mathfrak{M}}=6({\mathsf{T}}+{\mathfrak{C}}^{2}\eta_{*})
, and recall
f(t)
from (
3.2
). For any real number
t\in[0,\mathsf{T}]
, we define the spectral domain
{\mathcal{D}}_{t}={\mathcal{D}}_{t}(\mu_{t})
(see
Figure

3
) by

(3.28)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{t}\deq\left\{z\in\mathbb{H}:E_{t}+f(t)\leq{\rm Re}[z]\leq{\mathfrak{M}}-2t,\frac{1}{(\log n)n{\rm Im}[m_{t}(z)]}\leq{\rm Im}[z]\leq{\mathfrak{M}}-2t\right\}

Roughly speaking, the information of the Stieltjes transform
m_{t}(z)
on
{\mathcal{D}}_{t}
can be used to detect particles of distance
f(t)
from the spectral edge
E_{t}
. The following lemma collects some properties of the domain
{\mathcal{D}}_{t}
.

Lemma 3.4
.

Adopt
1.2
. Recall the domain
{\mathcal{D}}_{t}
from (
3.28
). For
n
large enough and any time
0\leq t\leq{\mathsf{T}}
, the following hold.

(1)

{\mathcal{D}}_{t}
is non-empty. For any
z=E_{t}+\kappa+\mathrm{i}\eta\in{\mathcal{D}}_{t}
, we have

(3.29)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{M}}\geq\kappa\geq f(t)\geq\frac{1}{n^{2/3}},\quad{\mathfrak{M}}\geq\eta\geq\frac{1}{n^{2}},\quad\kappa+\eta\leq{\mathsf{T}}^{2},\quad{\rm Im}[m_{t}(z)]\geq\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}\eta}{8\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}},

where
{\mathfrak{c}}
is from
2.14
, and

(3.30)

\displaystyle\frac{(\log n)^{7/2}}{n\sqrt{(\kappa+\eta)\eta}}\leq\frac{{\rm Im}[m_{t}(z)]}{\log n}.

(2)

If
z_{t}(u)=E_{t}+\kappa_{t}+\mathrm{i}\eta_{t}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{t}
, then
z_{s}(u)\in{\mathcal{D}}_{s}
for any
0\leq s\leq t
.

By the second part of
3.4
, if for any
u=z_{0}(u)\in{\mathcal{D}}_{0}
we define

(3.31)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{t}}(u):={\mathsf{T}}\wedge\sup\{t\geq 0:z_{t}(u)\in{\mathcal{D}}_{t}\},

then
z_{s}(u)\in{\mathcal{D}}_{s}
for any
0\leq s\leq{\mathfrak{t}}(u)
.

Proof of
3.4
.

For Item
1
,
{\mathfrak{M}}=6({\mathsf{T}}+{\mathfrak{C}}^{2}\eta_{*})\geq 2{\mathsf{T}}+2\sqrt{{\mathsf{T}}}+{\mathfrak{C}}^{2}\eta_{*}\geq 2t+E_{t}+f(t)
, where we used that
t\leq{\mathsf{T}}
,
E_{t}\leq 2\sqrt{t}\leq 2\sqrt{{\mathsf{T}}}
from the first statement in
2.15
, and
f(t)\leq f(0)={\mathfrak{C}}^{2}\eta_{*}
from (
3.2
). Moreover for any
z\in\mathbb{H}
with
E_{t}+f(t)\leq{\rm Re}[z]\leq{\mathfrak{M}}-2t
and
{\rm Im}[z]\leq{\mathfrak{M}}-2t
we have

(3.32)

\displaystyle{\rm Im}[m_{t}(z)]=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{{\rm d}\mu_{t}(x)}{|x-z|^{2}}\geq\int^{E_{t}}_{E_{t}-1}\frac{{\rm d}\mu_{t}(x)}{(2{\mathfrak{M}})^{2}}\geq\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}}{4{\mathfrak{M}}^{2}},

where the first inequality is from
|z-x|\leq 2{\mathfrak{M}}
for
x\in[E_{t},E_{t}-1]
; and the last inequality follows from the second statement of
2.15
(with
x=1
).
Thus for
n
large enough,
{\mathfrak{M}}-2t>1>1/(n{\rm Im}[m_{t}(z)]\log n)
, and
{\mathcal{D}}_{t}
is not empty.

If
E_{t}+\kappa+\mathrm{i}\eta\in{\mathcal{D}}_{t}
, then we have
\eta,\kappa\leq{\mathfrak{M}}
from the construction of
{\mathcal{D}}_{t}
in (
3.28
);
\kappa\geq f(t)\geq 1/n^{2/3}
from the definition (
3.2
);
and
\eta\geq 1/((\log n)n{\rm Im}[m_{t}(z)])\geq 1/((\log n)n(1/\kappa))\geq 1/n^{2}
(where the first statement is from the definition of
{\mathcal{D}}_{t}
(
3.28
); the second statement is from
\max(\supp\mu_{t})=E_{t}
and
2.1
that
|m_{t}(z)|\leq 1/\dist(z,\supp\mu_{t})\leq 1/\kappa
; the last statement follows from
\kappa\geq n^{-2/3}
) provided
n
is large enough. This gives the first two statement in (
3.29
). For the third statement, we recall from our assumption from (
3.1
) that
{\mathsf{T}}\geq(2^{60}/{\mathsf{b}}^{6})\sqrt{\eta}_{*}\geq 2^{10}{\mathfrak{C}}\sqrt{\eta_{*}}
(we recall that
{\mathfrak{C}}=2^{21}/{\mathsf{b}}^{2}
from (
2.15
) and
{\mathsf{b}},\eta_{*}<1
from
1.2
). Then
\kappa+\eta\leq 2{\mathfrak{M}}=12({\mathsf{T}}+{\mathfrak{C}}^{2}\eta_{*})\leq{\mathsf{T}}^{2}
(where we also used
{\mathsf{T}}\geq 100
from
1.2
.)
The last relation in (
3.29
) follows from (
2.54
) and
\kappa+\eta\leq{\mathsf{T}}^{2}
.

For (
3.30
), by the definition of
{\mathcal{D}}_{t}
, we have

(3.33)

\displaystyle\eta{\rm Im}[m_{t}(E_{t}+\kappa+\mathrm{i}\eta)]\geq\frac{1}{(\log n)n}.

There are two cases; we either have
\eta\geq(\log n)^{4}n^{-2/3}
or
\eta\leq(\log n)^{4}n^{-2/3}
. If
\eta\geq(\log n)^{4}n^{-2/3}
, then

(3.34)

\displaystyle\sqrt{(\kappa+\eta)\eta}{\rm Im}[m_{t}(E_{t}+\kappa+\mathrm{i}\eta)]\geq\sqrt{(\kappa+\eta)\eta}\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}\eta}{8\sqrt{\kappa+\eta}}=\frac{{\mathfrak{c}}}{8}\eta^{3/2}\geq\frac{(\log n)^{9/2}}{n},

provided
n
is large enough.
If
\eta\leq(\log n)^{4}n^{-2/3}
, then
\kappa\geq f(t)\geq(\log n)^{15}n^{-2/3}\geq(\log n)^{11}\eta
, and

(3.35)

\displaystyle\sqrt{(\kappa+\eta)\eta}{\rm Im}[m_{t}(E_{t}+\kappa+\mathrm{i}\eta)]\geq\sqrt{\frac{\kappa}{\eta}}\cdot\eta{\rm Im}[m_{t}(E_{t}+\kappa+\mathrm{i}\eta)]\geq\sqrt{\frac{\kappa}{\eta}}\cdot\frac{1}{n\log n}\geq\frac{(\log n)^{9/2}}{n}.

This finishes the proof of (
3.30
).

For Item
2
, for observe that
3.3
gives if
\kappa_{t}\geq f(t)
then
\kappa_{s}\geq f(s)
for
s\leq t
. Since
\eta_{s}\geq\eta_{t}
from (
3.7
), if
\eta_{t}\geq 1/((\log n)n{\rm Im}[m_{t}(z_{t})])
, then
\eta_{s}\geq\eta_{t}\geq 1/((\log n)n{\rm Im}[m_{t}(z_{t})])=1/((\log n)n{\rm Im}[m_{s}(z_{s})])
. Thus,
\Real z_{s}(u)
and
\Imaginary z_{s}(u)
satisfy the lower bounds required for
z_{s}(u)
to be in
\mathcal{D}_{s}
.

Next we prove by contradiction that
{\rm Re}[z_{s}(u)]\leq{\mathfrak{M}}-2s
and
{\rm Im}[z_{s}(u)]\leq{\mathfrak{M}}-2s
.
Otherwise, assume
\max\{{\rm Re}[z_{s}(u)],{\rm Im}[z_{s}(u)]\}>{\mathfrak{M}}-2s
. Recall our choice
{\mathfrak{M}}=6({\mathsf{T}}+{\mathfrak{C}}^{2}\eta_{*})\geq 6{\mathsf{T}}
and
E_{s}\leq 2\sqrt{s}\leq 2\sqrt{\mathsf{T}}
from the first statement of
2.15
. Then
{\mathfrak{M}}-2{\mathsf{T}}-E_{s}\geq{\mathfrak{M}}-3{\mathsf{T}}\geq{\mathfrak{M}}/2
(we used that
{\mathsf{T}}\geq 100
from
1.2
and
{\mathfrak{M}}-2{\mathsf{T}}\geq{\mathfrak{M}}/2
). Thus
\dist(z_{s}(u),(-\infty,E_{s}])\geq{\mathfrak{M}}/2
; so,
|m_{s}(z_{s})|\leq 1/(\dist(z_{s}(u),(-\infty,E_{t}])\leq 2/{\mathfrak{M}}
(from
2.1
), and
|z_{s}-z_{t}|=|t-s||m_{s}(z_{s})|\leq 2(t-s)/{\mathfrak{M}}
(here we used the definition of characteristic flow (
3.7
) and (
3.6
)). Then
{\rm Re}[z_{t}(u)]\geq{\rm Re}[z_{s}(u)]-2(t-s)/{\mathfrak{M}}
and
{\rm Im}[z_{t}(u)]\geq{\rm Im}[z_{s}(u)]-2(t-s)/{\mathfrak{M}}
. Therefore,
\max\{{\rm Re}[z_{t}(u)],{\rm Im}[z_{t}(u)]\}\geq\max\{{\rm Re}[z_{s}(u)],{\rm Im}[z_{s}(u)]\}-2(t-s)/{\mathfrak{M}}>{\mathfrak{M}}-2s-2(t-s)/{\mathfrak{M}}>{\mathfrak{M}}-2t
(we used our hypothesis
\max\{{\rm Re}[z_{s}(u)],{\rm Im}[z_{s}(u)]\}>{\mathfrak{M}}-2s
and
{\mathfrak{M}}\geq 1
), which contradicts the fact that
z_{t}(u)\in{\mathcal{D}}_{t}
. It follows that
\Real z_{s}(u)
and
\Imaginary z_{s}(u)
satisfy the upper bounds required for
z_{s}(u)
to be in
\mathcal{D}_{s}
, and
z_{s}(u)\in{\mathcal{D}}_{s}
.
∎

We next define the lattice on the upper half-plane
\mathbb{H}
given by

(3.36)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}=\left\{E+\mathrm{i}\eta\in\dom_{0}:E\in\mathbb{Z}/n^{8},\eta\in\mathbb{Z}/n^{8}\right\}.

Lemma 3.5
.

Adopt
1.2
.
For any
t\in[0,{\mathsf{T}}]
and
w\in\dom_{t}
, there exists some lattice point
u\in\mathcal{L}\cap z_{t}^{-1}(\dom_{t})
, such that

|z_{t}(u)-w|\leq n^{-5},

provided
n
is large enough.

Proof of
3.5
.

It follows from
3.4
that if
z_{t}(u)\in\dom_{t}
, then
u\in{\mathcal{D}}_{0}
, and
z_{t}(u)=u-tm_{0}(u)
with
u\in\dom_{0}
. In particular
|u-E_{0}|\geq f(0)=\mathfrak{C}^{2}\eta_{*}\geq 1/n^{2/3}
(by (
3.1
)), and

\del_{u}z_{t}(u)=1-tm_{0}^{\prime}(u)\leq 1+t/|u-E_{0}|^{2}\leq 1+{\mathsf{T}}n^{4/3}
(using
2.1
). Thus

z_{t}(u)
is Lipschitz in
u
with Lipschitz constant bounded by
(1+{\mathsf{T}}n^{4/3})
. Thus for any
w\in\dom_{t}
, there exists some lattice point
u\in\mathcal{L}\cap z_{t}^{-1}(\dom_{t})
, such that

|z_{t}(u)-w|\leq(1+{\mathsf{T}}n^{4/3})/n^{8}\leq n^{-5},

provided
n
is large enough.
∎

3.2.
Proof of
3.1

To prove
3.1
, we denote the Stieltjes transform of the empirical particle density by

(3.37)

\displaystyle\widetilde{m}_{t}(z)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\lambda_{i}(t)-z},\quad z\in\mathbb{H}.

By Itô’s formula,
\widetilde{m}_{s}(z)
satisfies a Burgers type stochastic differential equation,

(3.38)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\rm d}\widetilde{m}_{s}(z)&=-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{{\rm d}\lambda_{i}(s)}{(\lambda_{i}(s)-z)^{2}}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\langle{\rm d}\lambda_{i}(s),{\rm d}\lambda_{i}(s)\rangle}{(\lambda_{i}(s)-z)^{3}}\\
&=-\sqrt{\frac{2}{\beta n^{3}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{{\rm d}B_{i}}{(\lambda_{i}(s)-z)^{2}}-\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{(\lambda_{i}(s)-z)^{2}}\sum_{j:j\neq i}\frac{1}{\lambda_{i}(s)-\lambda_{j}(s)}+\frac{2}{\beta n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{{\rm d}s}{(\lambda_{i}(s)-z)^{3}}\\
&=-\sqrt{\frac{2}{\beta n^{3}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{{\rm d}B_{i}(s)}{(\lambda_{i}(s)-z)^{2}}+\widetilde{m}_{s}(z)\del_{z}\widetilde{m}_{s}(z){\rm d}s+\frac{2-\beta}{\beta n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{{\rm d}s}{(\lambda_{i}(s)-z)^{3}},\end{split}

where the last line follows from the fact that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{(\lambda_{i}(s)-z)^{2}}\sum_{j:j\neq i}\frac{1}{\lambda_{i}(s)-\lambda_{j}(s)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2n^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}^{n}\bigg(\frac{1}{(\lambda_{i}(s)-z)^{2}}\frac{1}{\lambda_{i}(s)-\lambda_{j}(s)}+\frac{1}{(\lambda_{j}(s)-z)^{2}}\frac{1}{\lambda_{j}(s)-\lambda_{i}(s)}\bigg)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2n^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}\frac{\lambda_{i}(s)-z+\lambda_{j}(s)-z}{(\lambda_{i}(s)-z)^{2}(\lambda_{j}(s)-z)^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}\frac{1}{(\lambda_{i}(s)-z)(\lambda_{j}(s)-z)^{2}}=\widetilde{m}_{s}(z)\del_{z}\widetilde{m}_{s}(z)+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{(\lambda_{i}(s)-z)^{3}}.

Plugging the characteristic flow (
3.5
) into (
3.38
), and using the chain rule, we have

(3.39)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\rm d}\widetilde{m}_{s}(z_{s})=\partial_{s}\widetilde{m}_{s}(z_{s})+\partial_{z}\widetilde{m}_{s}(z_{s})\partial_{s}z_{s}=-&\sqrt{\frac{2}{\beta n^{3}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{{\rm d}B_{i}(s)}{(\lambda_{i}(s)-z_{s})^{2}}\\
&+\del_{z}\widetilde{m}_{s}(z_{s})\left(\widetilde{m}_{s}(z_{s})-m_{s}(z_{s})\right){\rm d}s+\frac{2-\beta}{\beta n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{{\rm d}s}{(\lambda_{i}(s)-z_{s})^{3}}.\end{split}

Let
\mu_{t}=\mu_{0}\boxplus\mu_{\semci}^{(t)}
denote the free convolution between
\mu_{0}
with the rescaled semicircle distribution, and let
m_{t}
denote its Stieltjes transform. Then, we can integrate both sides of (
3.39
) from
0
to
t
to obtain

(3.40)

\displaystyle\widetilde{m}_{t}(z_{t})-m_{t}(z_{t})=\widetilde{m}_{t}(z_{t})-m_{0}(z_{0})=\widetilde{m}_{t}(z_{0})-\widetilde{m}_{0}(z_{0})=\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}\widetilde{m}_{s}(z_{s})ds=\int_{0}^{t}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{1}(s){\rm d}s+{\rm d}{\mathcal{E}}_{2}(s)\right),

where the error terms are

(3.41)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{1}(s)=

\displaystyle\left(\widetilde{m}_{s}(z_{s})-m_{s}(z_{s})\right)\del_{z}\widetilde{m}_{s}(z_{s})

(3.42)

\displaystyle{\rm d}{\mathcal{E}}_{2}(s)=

\displaystyle\frac{2-\beta}{\beta n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{{\rm d}s}{(\lambda_{i}(s)-z_{s})^{3}}-\sqrt{\frac{2}{\beta n^{3}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{{{\rm d}}B_{i}(s)}{(\lambda_{i}(s)-z_{s})^{2}}.

We remark that
\mathcal{E}_{1}
and
\mathcal{E}_{2}
implicitly depend on
u
, the initial value of the flow
z_{0}=u
.

We define the stopping time

(3.43)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sigma\deq{\mathsf{T}}\wedge\inf_{s\geq 0}\left\{\exists w\in\dom_{s}:\left|\widetilde{m}_{s}(w)-m_{s}(w)\right|\geq\frac{(\log n)^{7/2}}{n\sqrt{{\rm Im}[w]({\rm Re}[w-E_{s}]+{\rm Im}[w])}}\right\}.\end{split}

For
u\in{\mathcal{D}}_{0}
and
s\leq{\mathfrak{t}}(u)\wedge\sigma
, by the definition of the stopping time (
3.43
),
{\rm Im}[\widetilde{m}_{s}(w)]
and
{\rm Im}[m_{s}(w)]
are close. A more precise estimate is given in the following lemma.

Lemma 3.6
.

Adopt the notation and assumptions of
3.1
, and assume
n
is large enough. Given any
u\in{\mathcal{D}}_{0}
, recall
{\mathfrak{t}}(u)
from (
3.31
). For any
0\leq s\leq{\mathfrak{t}}(u)
, abbreviate
z_{s}=z_{s}(u)
; we have

(3.44)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left|{\rm Im}[\widetilde{m}_{s\wedge\sigma}(z_{s\wedge\sigma})]-{\rm Im}[m_{s\wedge\sigma}(z_{s\wedge\sigma})]\right|\leq\frac{{\rm Im}[m_{s\wedge\sigma}(z_{s\wedge\sigma})]}{\log n},\\
&{\rm Im}[\widetilde{m}_{s\wedge\sigma}(z_{s\wedge\sigma})]\leq 2{\rm Im}[m_{s\wedge\sigma}(z_{s\wedge\sigma})].\end{split}

Proof of
3.6
.

From the definition of
{\mathfrak{t}}(u)
as in (
3.31
),
z_{s}=z_{s}(u):=E_{s}+\kappa_{s}+\mathrm{i}\eta_{s}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{s}
for
0\leq s\leq t(u)
. From the definition (
3.43
) of
\sigma
, we have that

\displaystyle|\widetilde{m}_{s\wedge\sigma}(z_{s\wedge\sigma})-{m}_{s\wedge\sigma}(z_{s\wedge\sigma})|\leq\frac{(\log n)^{7/2}}{n\sqrt{(\kappa_{s\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{s\wedge\sigma})\eta_{s\wedge\sigma}}}\leq\frac{\text{Im}[{m}_{s\wedge\sigma}(z_{s\wedge\sigma})]}{\log n},

where we used (
3.30
) in the last inequality. The last statement in (
3.44
) also follows for
n
large enough.
∎

The following lemma states that before the stopping time
\sigma
, the largest particle is bounded by
E_{t}+f(t)
.

Lemma 3.7
.

Adopt the notation and assumptions in
3.1
, and assume
n
is large enough. For any
t\leq\sigma
(from (
3.43
)), we have

(3.45)

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}(t)\leq E_{t}+f(t).

Proof of
3.7
.

Fix a time
t\geq 0
and recall
{\mathcal{D}}_{t}
from (
3.28
). We notice that

(3.46)

\displaystyle\eta{\rm Im}[m_{t}(E_{t}+f(t)+\eta)]=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\eta^{2}\varrho_{t}(x){\rm d}x}{(x-E_{t}-f(t))^{2}+\eta^{2}},

is increasing in
\eta\geq 0
. When
\eta\rightarrow+\infty
, (
3.46
) goes to one; when
\eta\rightarrow 0
, (
3.46
) goes to zero. Therefore, there exists a (unique)
\eta_{t}>0
satisfying

(3.47)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(\log n)n}=\eta_{t}{\rm Im}[m_{t}(E_{t}+f(t)+\mathrm{i}\eta_{t})].

Then let
w_{t}=E_{t}+f(t)+\mathrm{i}\eta_{t}
. Recalling the definition of
{\mathcal{D}}_{t}
from (
3.28
), (
3.47
) and the monotonicity (
3.46
) imply that
w_{t}
is on the south-west corner of
{\mathcal{D}}_{t}
.

Next we claim that the bound

(3.48)

\displaystyle|\widetilde{m}_{t}(w_{t})-m_{t}(w_{t})|\leq\frac{(\log n)^{7/2}}{n\sqrt{{\rm Im}[w_{t}]({\rm Re}[w_{t}-E_{t}]+{\rm Im}[w_{t}])}},

implies that no
\lambda_{i}(t)
exists in the interval
[E_{t}+f(t)-\eta_{t},E_{t}+f(t)+\eta_{t}]
. Indeed, the estimate (
3.48
) implies

(3.49)

\displaystyle|\widetilde{m}_{t}(w_{t})-m_{t}(w_{t})|\leq\frac{(\log n)^{7/2}}{n\sqrt{{\rm Im}[w_{t}]({\rm Re}[w_{t}-E_{t}]+{\rm Im}[w_{t}])}}\leq{\rm Im}[m_{t}(w_{t})]=\frac{1}{(\log n)n{\rm Im}[w_{t}]},

where the second inequality is from
w_{t}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{t}
and (
3.30
); the last equality is from our construction (
3.47
).
Together with our choice of
\eta_{t}
from (
3.47
), it follows that

(3.50)

\displaystyle{\rm Im}[\widetilde{m}_{t}(w_{t})]\leq{\rm Im}[m_{t}(w_{t})]+|\widetilde{m}_{t}(w_{t})-m_{t}(w_{t})|\leq\frac{2}{(\log n)n{\rm Im}[w_{t}]}.

If there exists some
\lambda_{i}(t)
such that
\lambda_{i}(t)\in[E_{t}+f(t)-\eta_{t},E_{t}+f(t)+\eta_{t}]
, then the left-hand side of (
3.50
) is at least

\displaystyle{\rm Im}[\widetilde{m}_{t}(w_{t})]={\rm Im}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\lambda_{i}(t)-w_{t}}\right]=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{{\rm Im}[w_{t}]}{|\lambda_{i}(t)-w_{t}|^{2}}\geq\frac{{\rm Im}[w_{t}]}{n|w_{t}-\lambda_{i}(t)|^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{2n{\rm Im}[w_{t}]},

where in the first inequality we used that
{\rm Im}[w_{t}]=\eta_{t}>0
, so each term in the summation is positive; in the last inequality we used that
|w_{t}-\lambda_{i}(t)|^{2}\leq 2\eta_{t}^{2}
since
\lambda_{i}(t)\in[E_{t}+f(t)-\eta_{t},E_{t}+f(t)+\eta_{t}]
. This leads to a contradiction with (
3.50
), provided
n
is large enough.

For
t\leq\sigma
, the bound (
3.48
) holds (by the definition
3.43
), and so there is no
\lambda_{j}(t)
in the interval
[E_{t}+f(t)-\eta_{t},E_{t}+f(t)+\eta_{t}]
. At time
t=0
, we have
\lambda_{1}(0)\leq 0\leq f(0)
. Since
\lambda_{1}(t)
is continuous, and also
f(t)
and
E_{t}
are continuous (the first follows from the definition (
3.2
), and the second follows from the facts
E_{t}=\xi(t)-tm_{0}(\xi(t))
and
\xi(t)
is continuous from (
2.50
)) we conclude that
\lambda_{1}(t)\leq E_{t}+f(t)
for all
0\leq t\leq\sigma
.
∎

3.1
follows from the following statement and
3.7
. Its proof will occupy the remainder of this section.

Proposition 3.8
.

Adopt the notation and assumptions in
3.1
. There exists a constant
C=C({\mathsf{b}},{\mathsf{T}})>1
, with probability
1-Ce^{-(\log n)^{2}}
, we have
\sigma={\mathsf{T}}
.

Proof of
3.1
.

On the event
\sigma={\mathsf{T}}
,
3.7
implies that
\lambda_{1}(t)\leq E_{t}+f(t)
for
0\leq t\leq{\mathsf{T}}
. Thus
3.1
follows from
3.8
.
∎

In the rest of this section we prove
3.8
by analyzing the stochastic differential equation (
3.40
). The following proposition will be used to upper bound the error terms
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}(s)
and
{\mathcal{E}}_{2}(s)
.

Proposition 3.9
.

There exists a constant
C=C({\mathsf{b}},{\mathsf{T}})>1
and an event
\Omega
, measurable with respect to the Brownian paths
\{B_{1}(s),B_{2}(s),\cdots,B_{n}(s)\}_{0\leq s\leq{\mathsf{T}}}
, such that
\mathbb{P}[\Omega]\geq 1-Ce^{-(\log n)^{2}}
and the following holds. On
\Omega
, for any
u\in{\mathcal{L}}
(recall this lattice from (
3.36
)), denote
z_{s}(u)=E_{s}(u)+\kappa_{s}(u)+\mathrm{i}\eta_{s}(u)
. Then, for any
0\leq s\leq{\mathfrak{t}}(u)
(recall (
3.31
)), we have

(3.51)

\int_{0}^{s\wedge\sigma}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\beta n^{3}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{{\rm d}B_{i}(\tau)}{|z_{\tau}(u)-\lambda_{i}(\tau)|^{2}}{\rm d}\tau\leq\frac{C(\log n)^{2}}{n\sqrt{(\kappa_{s\wedge\sigma}(u)+\eta_{s\wedge\sigma}(u))\eta_{s\wedge\sigma}(u)}}.

and

(3.52)

\displaystyle\frac{|2-\beta|}{\beta}\int_{0}^{s\wedge\sigma}\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{|\lambda_{i}(\tau)-z_{\tau}(u)|^{3}}{\rm d}\tau\leq\frac{C\log n}{n(\kappa_{s\wedge\sigma}(u)+\eta_{s\wedge\sigma}(u))},

Proof of
3.9
.

For simplicity of notation, we write
\mathfrak{t}(u),z_{\tau}(u),\kappa_{\tau}(u),\eta_{\tau}(u)
as
\mathfrak{t},z_{\tau},\kappa_{\tau},\eta_{\tau}
, respectively.
By the definition of
\sigma
, for
\tau\leq\sigma
,
3.7
gives that

(3.53)

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}(\tau)\leq E_{\tau}+f(\tau).

To prove (
3.51
), we notice by
3.4
that
z_{\tau}(u)\in{\mathcal{D}}_{\tau}
for any
0\leq{\tau}\leq{\mathfrak{t}}
. We define a series of stopping times
0=t^{(0)}<t^{(1)}<t^{(2)}<\cdots<t^{(m)}={\mathfrak{t}}
,
as follows:

(3.54)

t^{(k)}={\mathfrak{t}}\wedge\inf\{{\tau}>t^{(k-1)}:(\kappa_{{\tau}}+\eta_{{\tau}})\eta_{{\tau}}<(\kappa_{t^{(k-1)}}+\eta_{t^{(k-1)}})\eta_{t^{(k-1)}}/2\},\quad k=1,2,3,\cdots,m,

where
m=m(u)
might depend on
u
. From (
3.29
),
\kappa_{t}
and
\eta_{t}
are finite and cannot be smaller than
1/n^{2}
; in particular,
\eta_{t}(\kappa_{t}+\eta_{t})\geq 2n^{-4}
, and so any
u\in\mathcal{L}
must satisfy
m=m(u)\leq 10\log n
.
We now apply the Burkholder–Davis–Gundy inequality to our stochastic integral. This allows us to bound the supremum of our stochastic integral over a time range by its quadratic variation with overwhelming probability. We refer to
[
15
, Theorem 3.2]
(see also
[
16
, Theorem 11.2.1]
) for a detailed introduction to the Burkholder–Davis–Gundy inequality. The quadratic variation of the left side of (
3.51
) at
s=t^{(k)}
is given by

(3.55)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\int_{0}^{t^{(k)}\wedge\sigma}\frac{2}{\beta n^{3}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\frac{{\rm d}{\tau}}{|z_{\tau}-\lambda_{k}({\tau})|^{4}}\leq\frac{\OO(1)}{n^{3}}\int_{0}^{t^{(k)}\wedge\sigma}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{|z_{\tau}-E_{\tau}-f(\tau)|^{2}}\frac{{\rm d}\tau}{|z_{\tau}-\lambda_{i}(\tau)|^{2}}\\
&=\frac{\OO(1)}{n^{2}}\int_{0}^{t^{(k)}\wedge\sigma}\frac{\text{Im}[\widetilde{m}_{\tau}(z_{\tau})]}{\eta_{\tau}((\kappa_{\tau}-f({\tau}))^{2}+(\eta_{\tau})^{2})}{\rm d}{\tau}\leq\frac{\OO(1)}{n^{2}}\int_{0}^{t^{(k)}\wedge\sigma}\frac{\text{Im}[m_{\tau}(z_{\tau})]}{\eta_{\tau}((\kappa_{\tau}-f({\tau}))^{2}+(\eta_{\tau})^{2})}{\rm d}{\tau},\end{split}

where in the first inequality we used that for
\tau\leq\sigma
we have
\lambda_{1}(\tau)\leq E_{\tau}+f(\tau)\leq\Real z_{\tau}
; in the second inequality we used the definition of
\widetilde{m}_{t}
from (
3.37
); and in the last inequality, we used (
3.44
) indicating that, for
{\tau}\leq\sigma
, we have
{\rm Im}[\widetilde{m}_{\tau}(z_{\tau})]\leq 2{\rm Im}[m_{\tau}(z_{\tau})]
.

Let
s=t^{(k)}\wedge\sigma
and
\widetilde{s}=\min\{s,\mathfrak{C}\sqrt{\eta_{*}}\}
. We can rewrite the integral in (
3.55
) as

(3.56)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{0}^{s}\frac{\text{Im}[m_{\tau}(z_{\tau})]}{\eta_{\tau}((\kappa_{\tau}-f({\tau}))^{2}+(\eta_{\tau})^{2})}{\rm d}{\tau}&\leq\int_{0}^{\widetilde{s}}\frac{\text{Im}[m_{\tau}(z_{\tau})]}{\eta_{\tau}(((\widetilde{s}-{\tau}){\rm Im}[m_{s}(z_{s})]\kappa_{\widetilde{s}}/\eta_{\widetilde{s}})^{2}+(\eta_{\tau})^{2})}{\rm d}{\tau}\\
&\qquad+\int_{\widetilde{s}}^{s}\frac{\text{Im}[m_{\tau}(z_{\tau})]}{\eta_{\tau}(((s-{\tau}){\rm Im}[m_{s}(z_{s})]\kappa_{s}/2\eta_{s})^{2}+(\eta_{\tau})^{2})}{\rm d}{\tau},\end{split}

where we used (
3.11
) to get the first term on the righthand of (
3.56
), and (
3.10
) to get the second term. The two terms on the righthand side of (
3.56
) can be bounded in the same way, so we will only study the second one.
Let
A:={\rm Im}[m_{\tau}(z_{\tau})]={\rm Im}[m_{s}(z_{s})]
; then
\eta_{\tau}=\eta_{s}+(s-\tau)A
(from (
3.7
)). Applying a change of variables replacing
s-\tau
by
\tau
, and then one replacing
A\tau
by
\tau
, we find

\displaystyle\int_{\widetilde{s}}^{s}\frac{\text{Im}[m_{\tau}(z_{\tau})]{\rm d}{\tau}}{\eta_{\tau}(((s-{\tau}){\rm Im}[m_{s}(z_{s})]\kappa_{s}/2\eta_{s})^{2}+(\eta_{\tau})^{2})}{\rm d}{\tau}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{A{\rm d}{\tau}}{(\eta_{s}+{\tau}A)(({\tau}A\kappa_{s}/2\eta_{s})^{2}+(\eta_{s}+{\tau}A)^{2})}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{{\rm d}{\tau}}{(\eta_{s}+{\tau})(({\tau}\kappa_{s}/2\eta_{s})^{2}+(\eta_{s}+{\tau})^{2})}.

If
\eta_{s}\geq\kappa_{s}
, then

(3.57)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{{\rm d}{\tau}}{(\eta_{s}+{\tau})(({\tau}\kappa_{s}/2\eta_{s})^{2}+(\eta_{s}+{\tau})^{2})}\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{{\rm d}{\tau}}{(\eta_{s}+{\tau})^{3}}\leq\frac{1}{2\eta_{s}^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{(\kappa_{s}+\eta_{s})\eta_{s}}.

If
\kappa_{s}\geq\eta_{s}
, we need to decompose the integral into three parts:

(3.58)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{{\rm d}{\tau}}{(\eta_{s}+{\tau})(({\tau}\kappa_{s}/2\eta_{s})^{2}+(\eta_{s}+{\tau})^{2})}=\int_{0}^{\eta^{2}_{s}/\kappa_{s}}(\cdots)+\int_{\eta^{2}_{s}/\kappa_{s}}^{\eta_{s}}(\cdots)+\int_{\eta_{s}}^{\infty}(\cdots)\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{\eta^{2}_{s}/\kappa_{s}}\frac{{\rm d}{\tau}}{\eta_{s}^{3}}+\int_{\eta^{2}_{s}/\kappa_{s}}^{\eta_{s}}\frac{{\rm d}{\tau}}{\eta_{s}({\tau}\kappa_{s}/2\eta_{s})^{2}}+\int_{\eta_{s}}^{\infty}\frac{{\rm d}{\tau}}{{\tau}({\tau}\kappa_{s}/2\eta_{s})^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{\kappa_{s}\eta_{s}}+\frac{4}{\kappa_{s}\eta_{s}}+\frac{2}{\kappa_{s}^{2}}\leq\frac{16}{(\kappa_{s}+\eta_{s})\eta_{s}}.\end{split}

By the same argument, the first term on the righthand side of (
3.56
) can be bounded by
16/((\kappa_{\widetilde{s}}+\eta_{\widetilde{s}})\eta_{\widetilde{s}})\leq 16/((\kappa_{s}+\eta_{s})\eta_{s})
(where the inequality is from the facts that
\widetilde{s}\leq s
and that
\kappa_{\tau}
and
\eta_{\tau}
are both decreasing function in the time
\tau
from (
3.7
) and (
3.8
)).

Thus, for
s=t^{(k)}\wedge\sigma
and any
p>1
, the Burkholder–Davis–Gundy inequality
[
15
, Theorem 3.2]
(see also
[
16
, Theorem 11.2.1]
), together with the upper bound of the quadratic variance (
3.55
), (
3.56
) and (
3.58
), yields
that there exists a constant
C_{0}>1
such that

(3.59)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in[0,t^{(k)}]}\left|\int_{0}^{{\tau}\wedge\sigma}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\beta n^{3}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{{\rm d}B_{i}({\tau})}{|z_{\tau}-\lambda_{i}({\tau})|^{2}}\right|^{2p}\right]\leq(72p)^{2p}\cdot\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\left(\frac{C_{0}}{n^{2}(\kappa_{t^{(k)}\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{t^{(k)}\wedge\sigma})\eta_{t^{(k)}\wedge\sigma}}\right)^{p}\Bigg].

By taking
p=(\log n)^{2}
in (
3.59
), there exists some
C_{1}>1
, such that with probability
1-e^{-2(\log n)^{2}}
we have

(3.60)

\sup_{0\leq{\tau}\leq t^{(k)}}\left|\int_{0}^{{\tau}\wedge\sigma}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\beta n^{3}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{{\rm d}B_{i}({\tau})}{|z_{\tau}-\lambda_{i}({\tau})|^{2}}\right|\leq\frac{C_{1}(\log n)^{2}}{n\sqrt{(\kappa_{t^{(k)}\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{t^{(k)}\wedge\sigma})\eta_{t^{(k)}\wedge\sigma}}}.

We define
\Omega
to be the event on which (
3.60
) holds for all
0\leq k\leq m
and all
u\in{\mathcal{L}}
. Since
m|{\mathcal{L}}|\leq|\mathcal{L}|\cdot 10\log n\leq n^{20}
, it follows from the discussion above that
\Omega
holds with probability
1-n^{20}e^{-2(\log n)^{2}}\geq 1-e^{-(\log n)^{2}}
, provided
n
is large enough. Therefore, for any
s\in[t^{(k-1)},t^{(k)}]
, the bounds (
3.60
) and our choice of
t^{(k)}
(
3.54
) (with the fact that
\eta_{s}
and
\kappa_{s}
are non-increasing in
s
) yield on
\Omega
that, for any
0\leq s\leq{\mathfrak{t}}(u)
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left|\int_{0}^{s\wedge\sigma}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\beta n^{3}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{{\rm d}B_{i}(\tau)}{|z_{\tau}-\lambda_{i}(\tau)|^{2}}\right|\leq\frac{C_{1}(\log n)^{2}}{n\sqrt{(\kappa_{t^{(k)}\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{t^{(k)}\wedge\sigma})\eta_{t^{(k)}\wedge\sigma}}}\leq\frac{2C_{1}(\log n)^{2}}{n\sqrt{(\kappa_{s\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{s\wedge\sigma})\eta_{s\wedge\sigma}}}.\end{split}

This finishes the proof of (
3.51
).

The left side of (
3.52
) is, following (
3.55
), bounded by

(3.61)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\int_{0}^{s\wedge\sigma}\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{|\lambda_{i}(\tau)-z_{\tau}|^{3}}{\rm d}\tau\leq\int_{0}^{s\wedge\sigma}\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{|\lambda_{i}(\tau)-z_{\tau}|^{2}|\kappa_{\tau}-f(\tau)+\mathrm{i}\eta_{\tau}|}{\rm d}\tau\\
&=\frac{1}{n}\int_{0}^{s\wedge\sigma}\frac{\text{Im}[\widetilde{m}_{\tau}(z_{\tau})]}{|\kappa_{\tau}-f({\tau})+\mathrm{i}\eta_{\tau}|\eta_{\tau}}{\rm d}{\tau}\\
&\leq\frac{\OO(1)}{n}\int_{0}^{s\wedge\sigma}\frac{\text{Im}[{m}_{\tau}(z_{\tau})]}{|\kappa_{\tau}-f({\tau})+\mathrm{i}\eta_{\tau}|\eta_{\tau}}{\rm d}{\tau}\leq\frac{\OO(1)}{n}\int_{0}^{s\wedge\sigma}\frac{{\rm Im}[m_{\tau}(z_{\tau})]}{(\kappa_{\tau}-f({\tau})+\eta_{\tau})\eta_{{\tau}}}{\rm d}{\tau}.\end{split}

where we used
\lambda_{k}(\tau)\leq\lambda_{1}(\tau)\leq E_{\tau}+f(\tau)
from (
3.53
), thus
|\lambda_{k}(\tau)-z_{\tau}|\geq|\kappa_{\tau}+E_{\tau}-f(\tau)+\mathrm{i}\eta_{\tau}|
in the first inequality; the definition of
\widetilde{m}_{t}(z)
for the second inequality; (
3.44
) in the third inequality; and
|\kappa_{\tau}-f(\tau)+\mathrm{i}\eta_{\tau}|\geq|\kappa_{\tau}-f(\tau)+\eta_{\tau}|/2
in the last inequality. Let
\widetilde{s}=\min\{s\wedge\sigma,{\mathfrak{C}}\sqrt{\eta_{*}}\}
; we can rewrite the integral in (
3.61
) as

(3.62)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s\wedge\sigma}\frac{{\rm Im}[m_{\tau}(z_{\tau})]}{(\kappa_{\tau}-f({\tau})+\eta_{\tau})\eta_{{\tau}}}{\rm d}{\tau}=\int_{0}^{\widetilde{s}}\frac{{\rm Im}[m_{\tau}(z_{\tau})]}{(\kappa_{\tau}-f({\tau})+\eta_{\tau})\eta_{{\tau}}}{\rm d}{\tau}+\int_{\widetilde{s}}^{s\wedge\sigma}\frac{{\rm Im}[m_{\tau}(z_{\tau})]}{(\kappa_{\tau}-f({\tau})+\eta_{\tau})\eta_{{\tau}}}{\rm d}{\tau}.

Using
3.3
, the two terms on the righthand side of (
3.62
) can be bounded in the same way as for (
3.56
); we will only study the second one.
Again let
A:={\rm Im}[m_{\tau}(z_{\tau})]={\rm Im}[m_{s\wedge\sigma}(z_{s\wedge\sigma})]
, so that
\eta_{\tau}=\eta_{s\wedge\sigma}+(s\wedge\sigma-\tau)A
. We can bound the second term on the righthand side of (
3.62
) as

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\int_{\widetilde{s}}^{{s\wedge\sigma}}\frac{\text{Im}[m_{\tau}(z_{\tau})]{\rm d}{\tau}}{\eta_{\tau}((s\wedge\sigma-{\tau}){\rm Im}[m_{s\wedge\sigma}(z_{s\wedge\sigma})]\kappa_{{s\wedge\sigma}}/2\eta_{{s\wedge\sigma}}+\eta_{\tau})}{\rm d}{\tau}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{A{\rm d}{\tau}}{(\eta_{s\wedge\sigma}+{\tau}A)(({\tau}A\kappa_{{s\wedge\sigma}}/2\eta_{{s\wedge\sigma}})+(\eta_{s\wedge\sigma}+{\tau}A))}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{{\rm d}{\tau}}{(\eta_{s\wedge\sigma}+{\tau})(({\tau}\kappa_{{s\wedge\sigma}}/2\eta_{{s\wedge\sigma}})+(\eta_{s\wedge\sigma}+{\tau}))}.

where we used (
3.10
) to bound the second term on the righthand side of (
3.62
) by the first line; in the second line we used the definition of
A
and applied a change of variables replacing
s\wedge\sigma-\tau
by
\tau
; in the last line we replaced
A\tau
with
\tau
.

If
\eta_{s\wedge\sigma}\geq\kappa_{s\wedge\sigma}
, then

(3.63)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{{\rm d}{\tau}}{(\eta_{s\wedge\sigma}+{\tau})(({\tau}\kappa_{{s\wedge\sigma}}/2\eta_{{s\wedge\sigma}})+(\eta_{s\wedge\sigma}+{\tau}))}\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{{\rm d}{\tau}}{(\eta_{s\wedge\sigma}+{\tau})^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{\eta_{s\wedge\sigma}}\leq\frac{2}{\kappa_{s\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{s\wedge\sigma}}.

If
\kappa_{s\wedge\sigma}\geq\eta_{s\wedge\sigma}
, we need to decompose the integral into two parts:

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{{\rm d}{\tau}}{(\eta_{s\wedge\sigma}+{\tau})(({\tau}\kappa_{{s\wedge\sigma}}/2\eta_{{s\wedge\sigma}})+(\eta_{s\wedge\sigma}+{\tau}))}=\int_{0}^{\eta_{s\wedge\sigma}}(\cdots)+\int_{\eta_{s\wedge\sigma}}^{\infty}(\cdots)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\eta_{s\wedge\sigma}}\frac{{\rm d}{\tau}}{\eta_{s\wedge\sigma}(\tau\kappa_{s\wedge\sigma}/2\eta_{s\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{s\wedge\sigma})}+\int_{\eta_{s\wedge\sigma}}^{\infty}\frac{{\rm d}{\tau}}{{\tau}({\tau}\kappa_{s\wedge\sigma}/2\eta_{s\wedge\sigma})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\log((\kappa_{s\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{s\wedge\sigma})/\eta_{s\wedge\sigma})}{\kappa_{s\wedge\sigma}}+\frac{2}{\kappa_{s\wedge\sigma}}\leq\frac{\OO(1)(\log n)}{\kappa_{s\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{s\wedge\sigma}},

where in the last inequality we used
(\kappa_{s\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{s\wedge\sigma})/\eta_{s\wedge\sigma}\leq n^{3}
(from (
3.29
)). The first term on the righthand side (
3.62
) can similarly be bounded by
\OO(1)\log n/(\kappa_{\widetilde{s}}+\eta_{\widetilde{s}})\leq\OO(1)\log n/(\kappa_{s\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{s\wedge\sigma})
(where the inequality is from the facts that
\widetilde{s}\leq s\wedge\sigma
, and that
\kappa_{\tau}
and
\eta_{\tau}
are both decreasing function in time
\tau
from (
3.7
) and (
3.8
)). This finishes the proof of (
3.52
).

∎

Proof of
3.8
.

Let us return to equation (
3.40
) for the difference between
\widetilde{m}_{t}(z)
and
m_{t}(z)
.
We plug (
3.51
) and (
3.52
) into (
3.40
), on the event
\Omega
as defined in
3.9
, to obtain

(3.64)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\widetilde{m}_{t\wedge\sigma}(z_{t\wedge\sigma})-m_{t\wedge\sigma}(z_{t\wedge\sigma})|&=\int_{0}^{t\wedge\sigma}\left|\widetilde{m}_{\tau}(z_{\tau})-m_{\tau}(z_{\tau})\right|\left|\partial_{z}\widetilde{m}_{\tau}(z_{\tau})\right|{\rm d}\tau\\
&+\OO\left(\frac{(\log n)^{2}}{n\sqrt{(\kappa_{t\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{t\wedge\sigma})\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}}}\right).\end{split}

For the first term on the righthand side of (
3.64
), using
2.1
and (
3.44
), we have

(3.65)

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{z}\widetilde{m}_{\tau}(z_{\tau})\right|\leq\frac{\text{Im}[\widetilde{m}_{\tau}(z_{\tau})]}{\eta_{\tau}}\leq\left(1+\frac{1}{\log n}\right)\frac{\text{Im}[{m}_{\tau}(z_{\tau})]}{\eta_{\tau}}.

We denote the quantity,

(3.66)

\displaystyle\beta(s):=\left(1+\frac{1}{\log n}\right)\frac{\text{Im}[{m}_{s}(z_{s})]}{\eta_{s}}\leq\frac{2\text{Im}[{m}_{s}(z_{s})]}{\eta_{s}},

and rewrite (
3.64
) as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left|\widetilde{m}_{t\wedge\sigma}(z_{t\wedge\sigma})-m_{t\wedge\sigma}(z_{t\wedge\sigma})\right|\leq&\int_{0}^{t\wedge\sigma}\beta(s)\left|\widetilde{m}_{s}(z_{s})-m_{s}(z_{s})\right|{\rm d}s+\frac{\OO(1)(\log n)^{2}}{n\sqrt{(\kappa_{t\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{t\wedge\sigma})\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}}}.\end{split}

By the Grönwall inequality, this implies for any
0\leq t\leq{\mathfrak{t}}(u)
that, on
\Omega
,

(3.67)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left|\widetilde{m}_{t\wedge\sigma}(z_{t\wedge\sigma})-m_{t\wedge\sigma}(z_{t\wedge\sigma})\right|&\leq\frac{\OO(1)(\log n)^{2}}{n\sqrt{(\kappa_{t\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{t\wedge\sigma})\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}}}\\
&\qquad+\int_{0}^{t\wedge\sigma}\beta(s)\left(\frac{\OO(1)(\log n)^{2}}{n\sqrt{(\kappa_{s}+\eta_{s})\eta_{s}}}+\right)e^{\int_{s}^{t\wedge\sigma}\beta(\tau){\rm d}\tau}{\rm d}s.\end{split}

For the function
\beta(\tau)
, using (
3.66
), we have the following estimates

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{s}^{t\wedge\sigma}\beta(\tau){\rm d}\tau&\leq\left(1+\frac{1}{\log n}\right)\int_{s}^{t\wedge\sigma}\frac{{\rm Im}[m_{\tau}(z_{\tau})]}{\eta_{\tau}}{\rm d}\tau=\left(1+\frac{1}{\log n}\right)\log\left(\frac{\eta_{s}}{\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}}\right),\end{split}

where in the last equality we used
\del_{\tau}\eta_{\tau}=-{\rm Im}[m_{\tau}(z_{\tau})]
, by taking the time derivative of (
3.7
). Thus,

(3.68)

\displaystyle e^{\int_{s}^{t\wedge\sigma}\beta(\tau)d\tau}\leq e^{\left(1+\frac{1}{\log n}\right)\log\left(\frac{\eta_{s}}{\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}}\right)}\leq\OO(1)\frac{\eta_{s}}{\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}},

where in the last inequality, we used (
3.29
), which gives
\log(\eta_{s}/\eta_{t\wedge\sigma})\leq 3\log n
, provided
n
is large enough.
Combining (
3.68
) with (
3.67
) and (
3.66
), we can bound the last term in (
3.67
) by

(3.69)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\mathcal{O}(1)\int_{0}^{t\wedge\sigma}\frac{\text{Im}[m_{s}(z_{s})]}{\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}}\left(\frac{(\log n)^{2}}{n\sqrt{(\kappa_{s}+\eta_{s})\eta_{s}}}\right){\rm d}s.\end{split}

Thanks to (
3.8
) in
3.2
, we have

(3.70)

\displaystyle(\kappa_{s}+\eta_{s})\eta_{s}\geq\left(\frac{\kappa_{t\wedge\sigma}}{\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}}\eta_{s}+\eta_{s}\right)\eta_{s}=\eta_{s}^{2}\frac{\kappa_{t\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}}{\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}}.

By plugging (
3.70
) into (
3.69
), we obtain

(3.71)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\sigma}\frac{\text{Im}[m_{s}(z_{s})]}{\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}}\left(\frac{(\log n)^{2}}{n\sqrt{(\kappa_{s}+\eta_{s})\eta_{s}}}\right){\rm d}s\leq\frac{(\log n)^{2}}{n\sqrt{\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}(\kappa_{t\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{t\wedge\sigma})}}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\sigma}\frac{\text{Im}[m_{s}(z_{s})]{\rm d}s}{\eta_{s}}\\
\leq\frac{(\log n)^{2}\log(\eta_{0}/\eta_{t\wedge\sigma})}{n\sqrt{\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}(\kappa_{t\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{t\wedge\sigma})}}\leq\frac{3(\log n)^{3}}{n\sqrt{\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}(\kappa_{t\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{t\wedge\sigma})}}.\end{split}

where in the first bound we used (
3.70
); in the second inequality we used
\del_{\tau}\eta_{\tau}=-{\rm Im}[m_{\tau}(z_{\tau})]
by taking the time derivative of (
3.7
); and in the last inequality, we used (
3.29
), which implies
\log(\eta_{0}/\eta_{t\wedge\sigma})\leq 3\log n
.

Combining the above estimates (
3.67
) and (
3.71
), we deduce on the event
\Omega
that, for any
u\in{\mathcal{L}}
and
0\leq t\leq\mathfrak{t}(u)
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\widetilde{m}_{t\wedge\sigma}(z_{t\wedge\sigma})-m_{t\wedge\sigma}(z_{t\wedge\sigma})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\OO(1)(\log n)^{3}}{n\sqrt{(\kappa_{t\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{t\wedge\sigma})\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}}}.

For
w\in{\mathcal{D}}_{t\wedge\sigma}
, from (
3.29
),
{\rm Im}[w]\geq 1/n^{2}
. Thus on
{\mathcal{D}}_{t\wedge\sigma}
,
both
\widetilde{m}_{t\wedge\sigma}(w)
and
m_{t\wedge\sigma}(w)
are Lipschitz with Lipschitz constant bounded by
n^{4}
(using
2.1
).
From
3.5
, for any
w\in{\mathcal{D}}_{t\wedge\sigma}
there exists some
u\in{\mathcal{L}}
with
|z_{t\wedge\sigma}(u)-w|\leq n^{-5}
. Thus we have

|\widetilde{m}_{t\wedge\sigma}(w)-\widetilde{m}_{t\wedge\sigma}(z_{t\wedge\sigma}(u))|\leq 1/n
and
|{m}_{t\wedge\sigma}(w)-m_{t\wedge\sigma}(z_{t\wedge\sigma}(u))|\leq 1/n
. Hence, for
n
large enough,

(3.72)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left|\widetilde{m}_{t\wedge\sigma}(w)-m_{t\wedge\sigma}(w)\right|&\leq\left|\widetilde{m}_{t\wedge\sigma}(z_{t\wedge\sigma}(u))-m_{t\wedge\sigma}(z_{t\wedge\sigma}(u))\right|+\left|\widetilde{m}_{t\wedge\sigma}(w)-m_{t\wedge\sigma}(z_{t\wedge\sigma}(u))\right|\\
&\qquad+\left|{m}_{t\wedge\sigma}(w)-m_{t\wedge\sigma}(z_{t\wedge\sigma}(u))\right|\\
&\leq\frac{\OO(1)(\log n)^{3}}{n\sqrt{(\kappa_{t\wedge\sigma}+\eta_{t\wedge\sigma})\eta_{t\wedge\sigma}}}+\frac{2}{n}\leq\frac{1}{10}\frac{(\log n)^{7/2}}{n\sqrt{({\rm Re}[w-E_{t\wedge\sigma}]+{\rm Im}[w]){\rm Im}[w]}}.\end{split}

uniformly for any
w\in{\mathcal{D}}_{t\wedge\sigma}
. Comparing (
3.72
) with the definition of the stopping time
\sigma
from (
3.43
), we conclude that on
\Omega
we have
\sigma={\mathsf{T}}
. Since
\mathbb{P}[\Omega]\geq 1-\mathcal{O}(e^{-(\log n)^{2}})
, this finishes the proof of
3.8
.
∎

3.3.
Edge Universality

Given what we have already done, our universality result
1.4
will be a quick consequence of the following coupling from
[
24
, Theorem 3.1]
. Before recalling that result, we need to introduce some notation.
Take
0<\omega_{1}<\omega_{0}/100
and fix two time scales

(3.73)

\displaystyle t_{0}=\frac{n^{\omega_{0}}}{n^{1/3}},\quad t_{1}=\frac{n^{\omega_{1}}}{n^{1/3}}.

We consider a pair of coupled solutions of the Dyson Brownian motion (
1.2
)
\{\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}(s)\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
and
\{\mu_{i}(s)\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
using the same Brownian motions as in
2.10
.

The Dyson Brownian motion
\{\mu_{i}(s)\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
starts from the eigenvalues of a Gaussian
\beta
ensemble. It is known that the law of
\{\mu_{i}(s)\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
is given by the
\beta
-ensemble and the empirical eigenvalue
(1/n)\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}\delta_{\mu_{i}(s)}
concentrates around the rescaled semicircle distribution (
[
11
, Theorem 2.4]
and also
[
12
,
10
,
13
]
),

(3.74)

\displaystyle\mu_{\rm sc}^{(1+s)}=\frac{\sqrt{4(1+s)-x^{2}}}{2(1+s)\pi}{\rm d}x,

We denote its right edge by
E_{\mu}(s)=2\sqrt{1+s}
.

We also assume there exists a small constant
c>0
, so that the empirical measure of the initial data of
\{\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}(s)\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
concentrates around a density
\widetilde{\varrho}_{0}(x)
with
E_{\lambda}(0)=\max\supp\widetilde{\varrho}_{0}(x)
, such that the following statements hold.

(1)

\widetilde{\varrho}_{0}(x)
has square root behavior close to
E_{\lambda}(0)
:

(3.75)

\displaystyle c\sqrt{E_{\lambda}(0)-x}\leq\widetilde{\varrho}_{0}(x)\leq c^{-1}\sqrt{E_{\lambda}(0)-x},\quad\text{for}\quad E_{\lambda}(0)-c\leq x\leq E_{\lambda}(0).

and

(3.76)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\varrho}_{0}(x)=\frac{\sqrt{E_{\lambda}(0)-x}}{\pi}\left(1+\OO\left(\frac{|E_{\lambda}(0)-x|}{t_{0}^{2}}\right)\right),\quad\text{for}\quad E_{\lambda}(0)-ct_{0}^{2}\leq x\leq E_{\lambda}(0).

(2)

We denote
\widetilde{\varrho}_{s}
the free convolution of
\widetilde{\varrho}_{0}
with the rescaled semicircle distribution
\mu_{\semci}^{(s)}
, its right edge by
E_{\lambda}(s)=\max\supp\widetilde{\varrho}_{s}
, and its classical eigenvalue locations by
\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}(s)=\gamma_{i;n}^{\widetilde{\varrho}_{s}}
as in (
2.14
). Then with probability
1-c^{-1}e^{-(\log n)^{2}}
, we have for all
0\leq s\leq t_{1}
that

(3.77)

\displaystyle|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}(s)-\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}(s)|\leq\frac{(\log n)^{1/c}}{i^{1/3}n^{2/3}},\quad\text{for}\quad 1\leq i\leq cn.

The assumptions (
3.75
) and (
3.76
) verify the conditions
[
24
, Equation (3.19) and (3.21)]
, and
[
24
, Theorem 3.1]
states that with high probability, the difference
\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}(s)-\mu_{i}(s)
is a constant up to a very small error of size
\OO(n^{-2/3-\delta})
.
1
1

1

We remark the result in
[
24
, Theorem 3.1]
is stated for
\beta=1
, but the coupling directly generalizes for
\beta\geq 1
. A detailed discussion in the setting of Dyson Brownian motion with
\beta\geq 1
(and general potential) can be found in
[
1
, Section 6]
.

Proposition 3.10

(
[
24
, Theorem 3.1]
)
.

There exists a small constant
\delta>0
such that the following holds for any real number
D>1
. We can couple the two Dyson Brownian motions
\{\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}(s)\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
with
\{\mu_{i}(s)\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
, such that for
n
large enough, with probability
1-n^{-D}
, we have

(3.78)

\displaystyle|(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}(t_{1})-E_{\lambda}(t_{1}))-(\mu_{i}(t_{1})-E_{\mu}(t_{1}))|\leq n^{-2/3-\delta},

for any finite
i\geq 1
(that is uniformly bounded in
n
).

Next we prove
1.4
using
3.10
. We recall the notation from the beginning of
Section

3.1
.
We also recall that
t\geq{\mathfrak{t}}=\max\{2{\mathfrak{B}}\sqrt{\eta_{*}},n^{-1/3+{\mathfrak{a}}}\}
(where
{\mathfrak{t}}
and
{\mathfrak{a}}
are from
1.4
). Fix a small real number
0<\omega<{\mathfrak{a}}/100
and let
t_{0}=t-n^{-1/3+\omega}
. Then

(3.79)

\displaystyle t_{0}\geq t/2\geq n^{-1/3+{\mathfrak{a}}}/2+{\mathfrak{B}}\sqrt{\eta_{*}}.

Then (
2.53
) in
2.14
gives for
x\in[E_{t_{0}},E_{t_{0}}-{\mathfrak{c}}(t_{0})]
that

(3.80)

\displaystyle\varrho_{t_{0}}(x)=(1+{\mathcal{E}}(E_{t_{0}}-x))\frac{\sqrt{{\mathcal{A}}({t_{0}})(E_{t_{0}}-x)}}{\pi},\qquad\text{where $\mathcal{E}$ satisfies $|{\mathcal{E}}(x)|\leq\frac{|x|}{{\mathfrak{c}}({t_{0}})}$},

and
{\mathfrak{c}}({t_{0}})
is from (
2.51
), and (
2.52
) gives
{\mathfrak{c}}({t_{0}})\geq 2^{-20}({\mathsf{b}}{t_{0}})^{2}
.
In the following we denote
{\mathcal{A}}:={\mathcal{A}}(t_{0})
.
Our assumption in
1.4
gives that
{\mathcal{A}}\in[\Theta^{-1},\Theta]
, and for
t_{0}\leq t\leq{\mathsf{T}}
that

(3.81)

\displaystyle\Theta^{-1}\sqrt{E_{t}-x}\leq\varrho_{t}(x)\leq\Theta\sqrt{E_{t}-x},\quad\text{for}\quad\quad E_{t}-\Theta^{-1}\leq x\leq E_{t}.

The next lemma states the difference
|{\mathcal{A}}-{\mathcal{A}}(t)|
is small (recall
{\mathcal{A}}={\mathcal{A}}(t_{0})
and
t_{0}=t-n^{-1/3+\omega}
)

Lemma 3.11
.

Adopt the assumptions in
1.4
, we have

(3.82)

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{A}}-{\mathcal{A}}(t)|\leq n^{-\omega},

provided
n
is large enough.

Proof.

Recall
\xi(s)
from (
2.50
), which satisfies the relation

(3.83)

\displaystyle m_{0}^{\prime}(\xi(s))=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{{\rm d}\mu_{0}(x)}{(x-\xi(s))^{2}}=\frac{1}{s}.

Recall from the first statement in (
2.52
) that
\xi(s)\geq({\mathsf{b}}s/20)^{2}
, and

(3.84)

\displaystyle|m_{0}^{(3)}(\xi(s))|=6\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{{\rm d}\mu_{0}(x)}{(x-\xi(s))^{4}}\leq\frac{6}{\xi(s)^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{{\rm d}\mu_{0}(x)}{(x-\xi(s))^{2}}=\frac{6}{s\xi(s)^{2}}\leq\frac{2400}{{\mathsf{b}}^{4}s^{5}},

where in the second statement we used the fact that
\xi(s)>\max(\supp\mu_{0})
(by (
2.50
)). By taking the derivative with respect to
s
on both sides of (
3.83
), we obtain

(3.85)

\displaystyle\xi^{\prime}(s)=\frac{1}{-s^{2}m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(s))}.

The time derivative of
g(s):=-s^{3}m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(s))/2=1/{\mathcal{A}}(s)
is bounded by

(3.86)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\del_{s}g(s)|&=\frac{1}{2}|\del_{s}(s^{3}m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(s)))|\\
&=\frac{1}{2}|3s^{2}m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(s))+s^{3}m_{0}^{(3)}(\xi(s))\xi^{\prime}(s)|\leq\frac{3|g(s)|}{s}+\frac{1200}{{\mathsf{b}}^{4}s|g(s)|},\end{split}

where in the last inequality we used (
3.84
) and (
3.85
)

By our assumption in
1.4
, we have
g(s)\in[\Theta^{-1},\Theta]
for
s\in[t_{0},t]\subset[{\mathfrak{t}}/2,{\mathsf{T}}]
. Then by (
3.86
), for
s\in[t_{0},t]
we have

(3.87)

\displaystyle|\del_{s}g_{s}|\leq\frac{3|g(s)|}{2s}+\frac{1200}{{\mathsf{b}}^{4}s|g(s)|}\leq\frac{3\Theta}{t_{0}}+\frac{4800\Theta}{{\mathsf{b}}^{4}t_{0}},\quad s\in[t_{0},t].

We conclude that

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{A}}(t)-{\mathcal{A}}(t_{0})|

\displaystyle=g_{t}^{-1}g_{t_{0}}^{-1}|g_{t}-g_{t_{0}}|\leq\Theta^{2}|t-t_{0}|\left(\frac{3\Theta}{t_{0}}+\frac{4800\Theta}{{\mathsf{b}}^{4}t_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{10^{4}\Theta^{3}}{{\mathsf{b}}^{4}}\frac{|t-t_{0}|}{t_{0}}\leq\frac{10^{4}\Theta^{3}}{{\mathsf{b}}^{4}}\frac{n^{-1/3+\omega}}{n^{-1/3+{\mathfrak{a}}}/2}\leq\frac{2\cdot 10^{4}\Theta^{3}}{{\mathsf{b}}^{4}}n^{\omega-{\mathfrak{a}}}\leq n^{-\omega},

provided
n
is large enough. Here, in the first statement we used
{\mathcal{A}}(s)=1/g(s)
; in the second statement we used
|g(s)|^{-1}\leq\Theta
and integrated (
3.87
) from
t_{0}
to
t
; the third statement is from basic algebra; the fourth statement uses that
t-t_{0}=n^{-1/3+\omega}
and
t_{0}\geq t/2\geq n^{-1/3+{\mathfrak{a}}}/2
from (
3.79
); and the last two inequalities follow from
\omega\leq{\mathfrak{a}}/100
and the fact that
n
is large enough.
This finishes the lemma.
∎

Proof of
1.4
.

For the Dyson Brownian motion
\{\lambda_{i}(s)\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
, we can rescale time by
{\mathcal{A}}^{2/3}
and space by
{\mathcal{A}}^{1/3}
. Specifically, define:
\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}(s)={\mathcal{A}}^{1/3}\lambda_{i}(t_{0}+{\mathcal{A}}^{-2/3}s)
. Then
\{\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}(s)\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
also satisfies Dyson’s Brownian motion:

(3.88)

\displaystyle{\rm d}\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}(s)=\frac{1}{n}\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq n\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}s}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}(s)-\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}(s)}+\Big(\displaystyle\frac{2}{\beta n}\Big)^{1/2}{\rm d}B_{i}(s).

Next we check that rescaled Dyson Brownian motion
\{\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}(s)\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
satisfies (
3.75
), (
3.76
) and (
3.77
).

We introduce the rescaled density
\widetilde{\varrho}_{0}(x)
, by setting (using (
3.80
))

(3.89)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\varrho}_{0}(x)=\frac{\varrho_{t_{0}}({\mathcal{A}}^{-1/3}x)}{{\mathcal{A}}^{1/3}}=\frac{\sqrt{(E_{\lambda}(0)-x)}}{\pi}\left(1+\OO\left(\frac{|E_{\lambda}(0)-x|}{t_{0}^{2}}\right)\right),\quad E_{\lambda}(0):={\mathcal{A}}^{1/3}E_{t_{0}},

for
x\in[E_{\lambda}(0)-{\mathcal{A}}^{1/3}{\mathfrak{c}}(t_{0}),E_{\lambda}(0)]
, where
{\mathfrak{c}}({t_{0}})\geq 2^{-20}({\mathsf{b}}{t_{0}})^{2}
.
We denote by
\widetilde{\varrho}_{s}
the free convolution of
\widetilde{\varrho}_{0}
with the rescaled semicircle distribution
\mu_{\semci}^{(s)}
. Then, by
2.4
,

(3.90)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\varrho}_{s}(x)=\frac{1}{{\mathcal{A}}^{1/3}}\varrho_{t_{0}+{\mathcal{A}}^{-2/3}s}({\mathcal{A}}^{-1/3}x),

and the right edge of
\widetilde{\varrho}_{s}(x)
is given by
\widetilde{E}_{s}={\mathcal{A}}^{1/3}E_{t_{0}+{\mathcal{A}}^{-2/3}s}
.
Moreover, (
3.81
) implies that for
0\leq s\leq 1
(from
t_{0}+{\mathcal{A}}^{-2/3}\leq t_{0}+\Theta\leq{\mathsf{T}}
) we have

(3.91)

\displaystyle\Theta^{-1}\sqrt{x/{\mathcal{A}}}\leq\widetilde{\varrho}_{s}(E_{\lambda}(s)-x)=\frac{\varrho_{t_{0}+{\mathcal{A}}^{-2/3}s}({\mathcal{A}}^{-1/3}(E_{\lambda}(s)-x))}{{\mathcal{A}}^{1/3}}\leq\Theta\sqrt{x/{\mathcal{A}}},

for
E_{\lambda}(s)-{\mathcal{A}}^{1/3}\Theta^{-1}\leq x\leq E_{\lambda}(s)
.
The relations (
3.89
) and (
3.91
) verify the conditions (
3.75
) and (
3.76
) provided
c\leq\min\{2^{-20}{\mathsf{b}}^{2}{\mathcal{A}}^{1/3},{\mathcal{A}}^{1/3}\Theta^{-1}\}
is small enough.

We denote the classical locations (recall from (
2.14
)) of
\widetilde{\varrho}_{s}(x)
by
\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}(s)=\gamma_{i;n}^{\widetilde{\varrho}_{s}}
.
The optimal edge rigidity estimate (from
1.3
) and bulk one (from
2.9
) give that there exists a constant
C=C({\mathsf{b}},{\mathsf{T}})>1
such that, with probability
1-Ce^{-(\log n)^{2}}
,

(3.92)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\lambda}_{1}(s)\leq E_{\lambda}(s)+\frac{(\log n)^{15}}{n^{2/3}},\quad\widetilde{\gamma}_{i+\lfloor(\log n)^{5}\rfloor}(s)-n^{-1}\leq\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}(s)\leq\widetilde{\gamma}_{i-\lfloor(\log n)^{5}\rfloor}(s)+n^{-1}.

for all
0\leq s\leq 1
(as
{\mathfrak{B}}\sqrt{\eta_{*}}\leq t_{0}\leq t_{0}+{\mathcal{A}}^{-2/3}\leq t_{0}+\Theta\leq{\mathsf{T}}
).

Since
\widetilde{\varrho}_{s}
has square root behavior as in (
3.91
), there exist constants
C_{0}=C_{0}(\Theta)>1
and
c>0
such that the classical locations satisfy

(3.93)

\displaystyle C_{0}^{-1}\frac{i^{2/3}}{n^{2/3}}\leq E_{\lambda}(s)-\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}(s)\leq C_{0}\frac{i^{2/3}}{n^{2/3}},\quad 0\leq s\leq 1,\quad 1\leq i\leq cn.

Thus, we have for any
1\leq i\leq j\leq cn
that

\displaystyle\frac{j-i}{n}

\displaystyle=\int_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{j}(s)}^{\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}(s)}\widetilde{\varrho}_{s}(x){\rm d}x\geq\frac{1}{{\mathcal{A}}^{1/2}\Theta}\int_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{j}(s)}^{\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}(s)}\sqrt{E_{\lambda}(s)-x}{\rm d}x=\frac{2(E_{\lambda}(s)-\widetilde{\gamma}_{j}(s))^{3/2}-(E_{\lambda}(s)-\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}(s))^{3/2}}{3{\mathcal{A}}^{1/2}\Theta}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{2(\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}(s)-\widetilde{\gamma}_{j}(s))}{3{\mathcal{A}}^{1/2}\Theta}\frac{(E_{\lambda}(s)-\widetilde{\gamma}_{j}(s))^{1/2}+(E_{\lambda}(s)-\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}(s))^{1/2}}{2}\geq\frac{(\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}(s)-\widetilde{\gamma}_{j}(s))}{3C_{0}{\mathcal{A}}^{1/2}\Theta}\frac{j^{1/3}}{n^{1/3}}

where the first statement is from the definition of classical locations (
2.14
); the second statement is from (
3.91
); the third statement is from performing integration; the fourth statement is from the bound
a^{3/2}-b^{3/2}\geq(a-b)(\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b})/2
; in the last statement is from (
3.93
). Thus we get

(3.94)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}(t)-\widetilde{\gamma}_{j}(t)&\leq\frac{3C_{0}{\mathcal{A}}^{1/2}\Theta(j-i)}{j^{1/3}n^{2/3}},\quad 0\leq s\leq 1,\quad 1\leq i\leq j\leq cn.\end{split}

Then, the second statement in (
3.92
) and (
3.94
) together imply that with probability
1-Ce^{-(\log n)^{2}}
we have for each
2(\log n)^{5}\leq i\leq cn
that

(3.95)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}(t)-\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}(t)|\leq|\widetilde{\gamma}_{i-\lfloor(\log n)^{5}\rfloor}(t)-\widetilde{\gamma}_{i+\lfloor(\log n)^{5}\rfloor}(t)|+2n^{-1}\\
&\leq\frac{3C_{0}{\mathcal{A}}^{1/2}\Theta\cdot 2(\log n)^{5}}{(i+(\log n)^{5})^{1/3}n^{2/3}}+2n^{-1}\leq\frac{(\log n)^{6}}{i^{1/3}n^{2/3}},\end{split}

provided
n
is large enough.

To address the remaining
1\leq i\leq 2(\log n)^{5}
, observe from (
3.92
) that, with probability
1-Ce^{-(\log n)^{2}}
, we have

(3.96)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\gamma}_{i+\lfloor(\log n)^{5}\rfloor}(s)-n^{-1}\leq\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}(s)\leq\widetilde{\lambda}_{1}(s)\leq E_{\lambda}(s)+\frac{(\log n)^{15}}{n^{2/3}},

so

(3.97)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}(s),\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}(s)\in\left[\gamma_{3\lfloor(\log n)^{5}\rfloor}(s)-n^{-1},E_{\lambda}(s)+\frac{(\log n)^{15}}{n^{2/3}}\right].

It follows that for each
1\leq i\leq 2(\log n)^{5}
we have

(3.98)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}(s)-\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}(s)|\leq\frac{(\log n)^{15}}{n^{1/3}}+|E_{\lambda}(s)-\gamma_{3\lfloor(\log n)^{5}\rfloor}(s)|+n^{-1}\\
&\leq\frac{(\log n)^{15}}{n^{1/3}}+\frac{3C_{0}{\mathcal{A}}^{1/2}\Theta\cdot 3(\log n)^{5}}{((\log n)^{5})^{2/3}n^{1/3}}+n^{-1}\leq\frac{(\log n)^{16}}{i^{1/3}n^{2/3}},\end{split}

provided
n
is large enough.
Here the first inequality follows from (
3.97
), and the second inequality follows from (
3.94
).
The two estimates (
3.95
) and (
3.98
) together give (
3.77
).
Thus
3.10
(with
(t_{0},t_{1})
there taken to be
(t_{0},{\mathcal{A}}^{2/3}n^{-1/3+\omega})
here) implies that we can couple the two Dyson Brownian motions
\{\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}(s)\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
(from (
3.88
)) with
\{\mu_{i}(s)\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
, such that with probability
1-n^{-D}
we have for any finite
i\geq 1
that

(3.99)

\displaystyle|(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}(t_{1})-E_{\lambda}(t_{1}))-(\mu_{i}(t_{1})-2\sqrt{1+t_{1}})|\leq n^{-2/3+\delta}.

where
t_{1}={\mathcal{A}}^{2/3}n^{-1/3+\omega}={\mathcal{A}}^{2/3}(t-t_{0})
, provided
n
is large enough.
Recall the relation
\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}(s)={\mathcal{A}}^{1/3}\lambda_{i}(t_{0}+{\mathcal{A}}^{-2/3}s)
, and
{\mathcal{A}}^{1/3}E_{\lambda}(s)=E_{t_{0}+{\mathcal{A}}^{-2/3}s}
; (
3.99
) then implies

(3.100)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}|({\mathcal{A}}^{1/3}(\lambda_{1}(t)-E_{t}))-(\mu_{1}(t_{1})-2\sqrt{1+t_{1}})|\leq n^{-2/3+\delta}.\end{split}

It was proved in
[
27
]
that as the number of particles
n
goes to infinity, the rescaled vector,

(3.101)

\displaystyle n^{2/3}(2\sqrt{1+t}-\mu_{1}(t))\rightarrow TW_{\beta},

converges to the Tracy-Widom
\beta
distribution, which is characterized by the stochastic Airy operator. A quantitative version of the convergence (
3.101
) follows from
[
23
, Theorem 1.2]
: Letting
F:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a bounded test function with bounded derivatives, we have for
n
is large enough that

(3.102)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{E}[F(n^{2/3}(2\sqrt{1+t}-\mu_{1}(t))]=\mathbb{E}_{TW_{\beta}}[F(\Lambda)]+\OO\left(n^{-\delta}\right),\end{split}

where
\Lambda
is sampled under the Tracy-Widom
\beta
distribution.
1.4
then follows from applying the test function
F
to (
3.100
), replacing
{\mathcal{A}}
by
{\mathcal{A}}(t)
(from
3.11
, their difference is bounded by
n^{-\omega}
), taking expectations, and plugging in (
3.102
).
∎

4.
Examples

In this section, we discuss some examples and applications of our optimal rigidity result
1.3
.

4.1.
Initial Data of Small Support

In this section, we study the example that the initial data
\mu_{0}=\mu_{0}^{(n)}
is supported on a small interval.

Assumption 4.1
.

Let
c\in(0,1)
be a real number, and assume that the initial data
\mu_{0}=\mu_{0}^{(n)}
satisfies
\supp\mu_{0}\subseteq[-c/2,c/2]
.

We notice that
\mu_{0}([c/2-x,c/2])=1
for any
x\geq c
. Then (after shifting by
c/2
)
1.2
is satisfied with
\eta_{*}=c
, and
{\mathsf{b}}=1/{\mathsf{T}}^{3}
. For the remainder of this section, we denote the free convolution of
\mu_{0}
with the rescaled semicircle distribution
\mu_{\semci}^{(t)}
by
\mu_{t}=\mu_{0}\boxplus\mu_{\semci}^{(t)}
. We also let
E_{t}^{-}=\min(\supp\mu_{t})
and
E_{t}^{+}=\max(\supp\mu_{t})
, for each
t\geq 0
.

We will show the following estimate on the
i
-th particle
\lambda_{i}(t)
of
\bm{\lambda}(t)
, using
1.3
.

Proposition 4.2
.

Adopt
4.1
, fix a real number
{\mathsf{T}}\geq 1
, and set
{\mathfrak{B}}=2^{60}{\mathsf{T}}^{18}
. Then, there exists a constant
C=C(c,{\mathsf{T}})>1
such that, with probability
1-Ce^{-(\log n)^{2}}
, we have for any time
{\mathfrak{B}}\sqrt{c}\leq t\leq{\mathsf{T}}
and index
1\leq i\leq n
that

(4.1)

\displaystyle|\lambda_{i}(t)-\gamma_{i}(t)|\leq\frac{(\log n)^{17}}{\min\{i,n-i+1\}^{1/3}n^{2/3}}.

As a consequence of
4.2
, we next state a result bounding the gaps between the first particles under the rescaled Dyson Brownian motion,

(4.2)

\displaystyle{\rm d}x_{i}(t)=\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq n\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}t}{x_{i}(t)-x_{j}(t)}+\Big(\displaystyle\frac{2}{\beta}\Big)^{1/2}{\rm d}B_{i}(t),\quad 1\leq i\leq n,

whose initial data is “sufficiently small.” This result will be used in the forthcoming work
[
3
]
.
We remark that similar result has been proven in
[
25
, Lemma 4.7]
. They do not require that the support of
\mu_{0}
is on a short interval. However, they require that the initial data
\mu_{0}
is close to a deterministic profile, with certain distance bounded by
n^{-\oo(1)}
.

Corollary 4.3
.

For any real number
B>1
, there exists a constant
c=B^{-34}2^{-120}>0
such that the following holds. Let
\bm{x}=(x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{n})\in\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{n}
be a sequence of real numbers such that
x_{1}-x_{n}<cn^{2/3}
. Let
\bm{x}(t)=\big(x_{1}(t),x_{2}(t),\ldots,x_{n}(t)\big)\in\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{n}
denote recaled Dyson Brownian motion (from (
4.2
)) with initial data
{\bm{x}}
, run for time
t
. Then, there exists a constant
C=C(B)>1
such that the following holds with probability at least
1-Ce^{-(\log n)^{2}}
. For all
t\in[B^{-1},B]
and
1\leq i\leq j\leq\lfloor n/2\rfloor
, we have

\displaystyle|x_{i}(tn^{1/3})-x_{j}(tn^{1/3})|\leq(24\pi)^{2/3}t^{1/2}(j^{2/3}-i^{2/3})+(\log n)^{20}i^{-1/3}.

An ingredient for the proof of
4.2
is the following lemma, which states that for time
t
much bigger than
c
, then
\mu_{t}
is close to a semicircle distribution.

Lemma 4.4
.

Adopt
4.1
. Fix
M=100
and a real number
t>0
satisfying
\sqrt{t}\geq 25cM^{2}
. Then the following four statements hold.

(1)

The spectral edge of
\mu_{t}
satisfies
|E_{t}^{\pm}\mp 2\sqrt{t}|\leq 2c
.

(2)

For
-2\sqrt{t}\sqrt{1-(2M)^{-2}}\leq y\leq 2\sqrt{t}\sqrt{1-(2M)^{-2}}
, we have

(4.3)

\displaystyle\frac{2}{3}\frac{\sqrt{4t-y^{2}}}{2\pi t}\leq\varrho_{t}(y)\leq\frac{3}{2}\frac{\sqrt{4t-y^{2}}}{2\pi t}.

(3)

For
2\sqrt{t}\sqrt{1-(2M)^{-2}}\leq y\leq E_{t}^{+}
, we have

(4.4)

\displaystyle\frac{2}{3\pi}\sqrt{\frac{E^{+}_{t}-y}{t^{3/2}}}\leq\varrho_{t}(y)\leq\frac{3}{2\pi}\sqrt{\frac{E^{+}_{t}-y}{t^{3/2}}},

and the similar statement holds for
E_{t}^{-}\leq y\leq-\sqrt{2t}\sqrt{1-(2M)^{-2}}
,

(4.5)

\displaystyle\frac{2}{3\pi}\sqrt{\frac{y-E^{-}_{t}}{t^{3/2}}}\leq\varrho_{t}(y)\leq\frac{3}{2\pi}\sqrt{\frac{y-E^{-}_{t}}{t^{3/2}}}.

(4)

We denote the inverted cumulative density function of
\varrho_{t}
by
\gamma_{t}
(recall from (
2.13
)). Then, for any
0\leq y\leq y^{\prime}\leq 4/7
, we have

(4.6)

\displaystyle\gamma_{t}(y)-\gamma_{t}(y^{\prime})\leq(24\pi)^{2/3}t^{1/2}(y^{\prime 2/3}-y^{2/3}).

Proof of
Item

1
in
4.4
.

We first prove
|E_{t}^{\pm}\mp 2\sqrt{t}|\leq 2c
. We will only prove the estimate for
E_{t}^{+}
, as the proof for
E_{t}^{-}
is very similar. We recall
\xi(t)
from (
2.50
) and that
\mu_{0}
is supported on
[-c/2,c/2]
. Hence,

(4.7)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(\xi(t)+c/2)^{2}}\leq m_{0}^{\prime}(\xi(t))=\int_{-c/2}^{c/2}\frac{{\rm d}\mu_{0}(x)}{(\xi(t)-x)^{2}}=\frac{1}{t}\leq\frac{1}{(\xi(t)-c/2)^{2}},

where the first and last inequality follows from
4.1
that
\mu_{0}
is supported on
[-c/2,c/2]
; the second and third equation is the definition of
\xi(t)
from (
2.50
). It follows that

(4.8)

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}-c/2\leq\xi(t)\leq\sqrt{t}+c/2.

By
Item

5
in
2.3
, the spectral edge
E_{t}^{+}
is given by

(4.9)

\displaystyle\begin{split}E_{t}^{+}=\xi(t)-tm_{0}(\xi(t))&=\xi(t)-t\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{{\rm d}\mu_{0}(x)}{x-\xi(t)}\\
&\leq\xi(t)+t\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{(\xi(t)+c/2){\rm d}\mu_{0}(x)}{(x-\xi(t))^{2}}=2\xi(t)+c/2\leq 2\sqrt{t}+2c,\end{split}

where in the second line we first used that
\mu_{0}
is supported on
[-c/2,c/2]
, and then that
\xi(t)\leq\sqrt{t}+c/2
from (
4.8
). Through very similar reasoning (which we omit), we also have the lower bound
E_{t}^{+}\geq 2\sqrt{t}-2c
, thereby establishing the bound
|E_{t}^{+}-2\sqrt{t}|\leq 2c
.
∎

Next we recall the relation between
\varrho_{t}(y)
and the Stieltjes transform
m_{0}
of
\mu_{0}
from
2.3
. Recalling the region
\Lambda_{t}
from (
2.7
) and the function
v_{t}
from (
2.9
),
Item

1
in
2.3
indiciates that we can parametrize
\del\Lambda_{t}
as
\{u+\mathrm{i}v_{t}(u):u\in{\mathbb{R}}\}
; we also have

(4.10)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t}\geq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{{\rm d}\mu_{0}(x)}{|x-(u+\mathrm{i}v_{t}(u))|^{2}}.

Thus, from
2.2
, for any
y\in{\mathbb{R}}
, there exists
w=w_{t}(u)=u+\mathrm{i}v_{t}(u)\in\del\Lambda_{t}
such that
y=w-tm_{0}(w)
.
From
Item

4
in
2.3
,
y\in{\mathbb{R}}
satisfies
\varrho_{t}(y)>0
if and only if
w\in\del\Lambda_{t}\cap\mathbb{H}
, i.e.,
{\rm Im}[w]>0
. Moreover, if this is the case,
Item

1
and
Item

4
in
2.3
give

(4.11)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{{\rm d}\mu_{0}(x)}{|x-(u+\mathrm{i}v_{t}(u))|^{2}},\quad\varrho_{t}(y)=\frac{{\rm Im}[w]}{\pi t}.

Since
\mu_{0}
is supported on
[-c/2,c/2]
by
4.1
, we get from (
4.10
) that

(4.12)

\displaystyle\sqrt{t}\leq\max\{|u+\mathrm{i}v_{t}(u)-c/2|,|u+\mathrm{i}v_{t}(u)+c/2|\}

We conclude that
\sqrt{t}-c/2\leq|w|=|u+\mathrm{i}v_{t}(u)|
, and hence for any
w\in\del\Lambda_{t}
that

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\left|m_{0}(w)+\frac{1}{w}\right|=\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{{\rm d}\mu_{0}(x)}{x-w}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{{\rm d}\mu_{0}(x)}{w}\right|\leq\int_{-c/2}^{c/2}\frac{|x|{\rm d}\mu_{0}(x)}{|x-w||w|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c/2}{(\sqrt{t}-c/2)(\sqrt{t}-c)}=\frac{c}{2t}\frac{1}{(1-c/(2\sqrt{t}))(1-c/\sqrt{t})}\leq\frac{c}{2t}\frac{1}{(1-1/6)(1-1/3)}\leq\frac{c}{t}.

where the first statement is from the definition of
m_{0}(w)
; the second statement holds by taking absolute value of the integrand and using
\supp\mu_{0}\subset[-c/2,c/2]
from
4.1
; the third statement follows from the bounds
|x|\leq c/2
and
|w|\geq\sqrt{t}-c/2
; the fourth statement follows from dividing
t
in both numerator and denominator; and in the fifth statement follows from the bound
\sqrt{t}\geq 3c
imposed in the assumption of
4.4
. In what follows, we define for any
z\in\del\Lambda_{t}
,

(4.13)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}(z)=t(m_{0}(z)+1/z),\qquad\text{so that $|{\mathcal{E}}(z)|\leq c$}.

Then,

(4.14)

\displaystyle y=w-tm_{0}(w)=w+\frac{t}{w}-{\mathcal{E}}(w),\qquad\text{so that}\qquad w^{2}-(y+{\mathcal{E}}(w))w+t=0.

By solving (
4.14
), we deduce

(4.15)

\displaystyle w=\frac{(y+{\mathcal{E}}(w))+\sqrt{(y+{\mathcal{E}}(w))^{2}-4t}}{2},

where the branch of the square root will be chosen such that
{\rm Im}[w]\geq 0
(see
4.18
below).

Proof of
Item

2
in
4.4
.

We must estimate
\varrho_{t}(y)
for

-2\sqrt{t}\sqrt{1-(2M)^{-2}}\leq y\leq 2\sqrt{t}\sqrt{1-(2M)^{-2}}
.
Notice that
-2\sqrt{t}\sqrt{1-(2M)^{-2}}\leq y\leq 2\sqrt{t}\sqrt{1-(2M)^{-2}}
is equivalent to
4t-y^{2}\geq t/M^{2}
, which implies that

(4.16)

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{t}-y\geq\frac{\sqrt{t}}{4M^{2}},\quad y+2\sqrt{t}\geq\frac{\sqrt{t}}{4M^{2}}.

We have proven in
Item

1
that
