|E_{t}^{\pm}\mp 2\sqrt{t}|\leq 2c
, which together with the assumption
\sqrt{t}\geq 25cM^{2}
from
4.4
implies that
y\in[E_{t}^{-},E_{t}^{+}]
. Recall
w\in\del\Lambda_{t}
with
w-tm_{0}(w)=y\in\mathbb{R}
. From (
4.15
) and (
4.13
), we have

(4.17)

\displaystyle 0\leq{\rm Im}[w]=\frac{{\rm Im}[{\mathcal{E}}(w)]+{\rm Im}[\sqrt{(y+{\mathcal{E}}(w))^{2}-4t}]}{2},\quad\text{so that}\quad{\rm Im}[\sqrt{(y+{\mathcal{E}}(w))^{2}-4t}]\geq-c.

Next we show that we should take the square root branch in (
4.15
) with positive imaginary part, and

(4.18)

\displaystyle|\sqrt{(y+{\mathcal{E}}(w))^{2}-4t}-\sqrt{y^{2}-4t}|\leq\frac{4c\sqrt{t}}{\sqrt{|y^{2}-4t|}}.

We first notice that

(4.19)

\displaystyle|((y+{\mathcal{E}}(w))^{2}-4t)-(y^{2}-4t)|\leq|{\mathcal{E}}(w)||2y+{\mathcal{E}}(w)|\leq c(4\sqrt{t}-\sqrt{t}/(2M^{2})+c)\leq 4c\sqrt{t}\leq t/{2M^{2}}.

where in the second inequality we used
|{\mathcal{E}}(w)|\leq c
from (
4.13
) and (
4.16
); in the last two inequalities we used that
c\leq\sqrt{t}/(8M^{2})
following from our assumption in
4.4
. There are two choices for
\sqrt{(y+{\mathcal{E}}(w))^{2}-4t}
; its imaginary part either can either be nonnegative or negative. In the latter case
{\rm Im}[\sqrt{(y+{\mathcal{E}}(w))^{2}-4t}]<0
, we have

(4.20)

\displaystyle{\rm Im}[\sqrt{(y+{\mathcal{E}}(w))^{2}-4t}]\leq-\sqrt{4t-y^{2}-t/(2M^{2})}\leq-\sqrt{t/4M^{2}}=-\frac{\sqrt{t}}{2M}<-c,

where in the first inequality we used (
4.19
); in the second inequality we used
4t-y^{2}\geq t/M^{2}
(see above (
4.16
)); and in the last inequality we used
c\leq\sqrt{t}/(8M^{2})
following from our assumption in
4.4
. Since (
4.20
) contradicts with (
4.17
), we conclude that
{\rm Im}[\sqrt{(y+{\mathcal{E}}(w))^{2}-4t}]\geq 0
. Then (
4.18
) follows from

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}|\sqrt{(y+{\mathcal{E}}(w))^{2}-4t}-\sqrt{y^{2}-4t}|=\left|\frac{((y+{\mathcal{E}}(w))^{2}-4t)-(y^{2}-4t)}{\sqrt{(y+{\mathcal{E}}(w))^{2}-4t}+\sqrt{y^{2}-4t}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4c\sqrt{t}}{|{\rm Im}[\sqrt{(y+{\mathcal{E}}(w))^{2}-4t}+\sqrt{y^{2}-4t}]|}\leq\frac{4c\sqrt{t}}{|{\rm Im}[\sqrt{y^{2}-4t}]|}=\frac{4c\sqrt{t}}{\sqrt{|y^{2}-4t|}}.

where in the first inequality we used (
4.19
); in the second we used
{\rm Im}[\sqrt{(y+{\mathcal{E}}(w))^{2}-4t}]\geq 0
from the above discussion; and in the last inequality we used that
y^{2}-4t<0
(see above (
4.16
)).

Now we can use (
4.18
) to estimate
w
in (
4.15
). It follows from (
4.15
) that

(4.21)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\left|w-\frac{y+\sqrt{y^{2}-4t}}{2}\right|\leq\frac{c}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left|\sqrt{(y+{\mathcal{E}}(w))^{2}-4t}-\sqrt{y^{2}-4t}\right|\leq\frac{c}{2}+\frac{2c\sqrt{t}}{\sqrt{|y^{2}-4t|}}\leq\frac{(4M+1)c}{2}.\end{split}

where we used that
|{\mathcal{E}}(w)|\leq c
in the first inequality; we used (
4.18
) in the second inequality; and we used
4t-y^{2}\geq t/M^{2}
(from above (
4.16
)) for the last inequality.

We conclude from plugging (
4.21
) into the second statement of (
4.11
) that

(4.22)

\displaystyle\left|\varrho_{t}(y)-\frac{\sqrt{4t-y^{2}}}{2\pi t}\right|\leq\frac{(4M+1)c}{2\pi t}\leq\frac{1}{3}\frac{\sqrt{t}/M}{2\pi t}\leq\frac{1}{3}\frac{\sqrt{4t-y^{2}}}{2\pi t},

where we used
\sqrt{t}\geq 25M^{2}c\geq 3cM(4M+1)
following from our assumption in
4.4
and
4t-y^{2}\geq t/M^{2}
(from above (
4.16
)). This finishes the proof of (
4.3
).
∎

Proof of
Item

3
in
4.4
.

The proof of (
4.5
) is exactly the same at that of (
4.4
), so we only establish the latter. We have the estimate for
m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))
,

(4.23)

\displaystyle\frac{2}{(\sqrt{t}+c)^{3}}\leq\frac{2}{(\xi(t)+c/2)^{3}}\leq-m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))=\int_{-c/2}^{c/2}\frac{2{\rm d}\mu_{0}(x)}{(\xi(t)-x)^{3}}\leq\frac{2}{(\xi(t)-c/2)^{3}}\leq\frac{2}{(\sqrt{t}-c)^{3}}.

where the first and last inequalities follow from the estimate on
\xi(t)
from (
4.8
); the second and fourth inequalities follow from the fact that
\supp\mu_{0}\subset[-c/2,c/2]
.

Recall from (
4.9
) that
E_{t}^{+}\leq 2\sqrt{t}+2c
, so
2\sqrt{t}\sqrt{1-(2M)^{-2}}\leq y<E_{t}^{+}
implies that

(4.24)

\displaystyle|y^{2}-4t|\leq\max\{t/M^{2},(2\sqrt{t}+2c)^{2}-4t\}=\max\{t/M^{2},8\sqrt{t}c+4c^{2}\}\leq t/M^{2},

and

(4.25)

\displaystyle-\sqrt{t}/(2M^{2})\leq y-2\sqrt{t}\leq 2c\leq\sqrt{t}/(2M^{2}),

where in the last inequalities (of both) we used
c\leq\sqrt{t}/(25M^{2})
from our assumption in
4.4
.
Using the relation (
4.15
)

(4.26)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|w-\sqrt{t}|&\leq\frac{|y-2\sqrt{t}+{\mathcal{E}}(w)|+\sqrt{|(y+{\mathcal{E}}(w))^{2}-4t|}}{2}\\
&\leq\frac{(\sqrt{t}/2M^{2}+|{\mathcal{E}}(w)|)+\sqrt{t/M^{2}+|{\mathcal{E}}(w)(2y+{\mathcal{E}}(w))|}}{2}\leq\frac{2\sqrt{t}}{M},\end{split}

where we used (
4.24
) and (
4.25
) for the second inequality; and for the last inequality we used
|{\mathcal{E}}(w)|\leq c
(from (
4.13
)), implying that

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{E}}(w)(2y+{\mathcal{E}}(w))|\leq c|4\sqrt{t}+4c+c|\leq\frac{8\sqrt{t}}{25M^{2}}\left(\sqrt{t}+\frac{\sqrt{t}}{10M^{2}}\right)\leq\frac{t}{M^{2}},

which holds since
2y\leq 4\sqrt{t}+4c
(by (
4.25
)),
M=100
, and
c\leq\sqrt{t}/25M^{2}
from our assumption in
4.4
.

We can now Taylor expand
m_{0}(w)
around
\xi(t)
:

(4.27)

\displaystyle\begin{split}m_{0}(w)&=m_{0}(\xi(t))+m_{0}^{\prime}(\xi(t))(w-\xi(t))+m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))\frac{(w-\xi(t))^{2}}{2}+\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(w)\frac{(w-\xi(t))^{3}}{3!}\\
&=m_{0}(\xi(t))+\frac{(w-\xi(t))}{t}+m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))\frac{(w-\xi(t))^{2}}{2}+\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(w)\frac{(w-\xi(t))^{3}}{3!},\end{split}

where we used
m_{0}^{\prime}(\xi(t))=1/t
from the definition (
2.50
) of
\xi(t)
, and for
w
satisfying (
4.26
) the Taylor remainder satisfies

(4.28)

\displaystyle|\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(w)|\leq\max_{|z-\sqrt{t}|\leq 2\sqrt{t}/M}|m_{0}^{(3)}(z)|\leq\max_{|z-\sqrt{t}|\leq 2\sqrt{t}/M}\frac{6}{||z|-c/2|^{4}}\leq\frac{6}{|\sqrt{t}-2\sqrt{t}/M-c/2|^{4}},

where we used
2.1
with
(A,p)=(1,3)
and
\dist(z,\supp\mu_{0})\geq||z|-c/2|
for the second inequality; and
|z|\geq\sqrt{t}-2\sqrt{t}/M
for the last inequality.

We can now use (
4.27
) to solve for the curve
\{u+\mathrm{i}v_{t}(u):u\in{\mathbb{R}}\}=\del\Lambda_{t}
in a small neighborhood of
\xi(t)
. In particular, setting
y=E^{+}_{t}-x=w-tm_{0}(w)
, we obtain by rearranging (
4.27
) that

(4.29)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathbb{R}}\ni x&=(\xi(t)-tm_{0}(\xi(t)))-(w-tm_{0}(w))=tm_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))\frac{(w-\xi(t))^{2}}{2}+t\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(w)\frac{(w-\xi(t))^{3}}{3!}\\
&=\left(tm_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))+t\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(w)\frac{w-\xi(t)}{3}\right)\frac{(w-\xi(t))^{2}}{2}.\end{split}

Hence,

(4.30)

\displaystyle w-\xi(t)=\mathrm{i}\sqrt{\frac{2x}{-tm_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))}}\left(1+\frac{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(w)(w-\xi(t))}{3m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))}\right)^{-1/2}.

Using (
4.23
), for the denominator in (
4.30
), we have

(4.31)

\displaystyle\frac{3}{4}\frac{2}{\sqrt{t}}\leq\frac{2t}{(\sqrt{t}+c)^{3}}\leq-tm_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))\leq\frac{2t}{(\sqrt{t}-c)^{3}}\leq\frac{2}{\sqrt{t}}\frac{4}{3},

where we used that
c\leq\sqrt{t}/25M^{2}
and
M=100
from our assumption in
4.4
.
Using (
4.26
) and (
4.8
), we have the bound
|w-\xi(t)|\leq 2\sqrt{t}/M+c/2
. This, together with (
4.28
) and (
4.31
), bounds the error term in (
4.30
) by

(4.32)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left|\frac{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(w)(w-\xi(t))}{3m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))}\right|&\leq\left(\frac{2\sqrt{t}}{M}+\frac{c}{2}\right)\frac{6}{|\sqrt{t}-2\sqrt{t}/M-c/2|^{4}}\frac{(\sqrt{t}+c)^{3}}{6}\\
&\leq\left(\frac{2}{M}+\frac{1}{25M^{2}}\right)\frac{(1+1/25M^{2})^{3}}{|1-2/M-1/50M^{2}|^{4}}\leq\frac{1}{3}\end{split}

where we also used that
c\leq\sqrt{t}/25M^{2}
and
M=100
from our assumption in
4.4
. Then, plugging (
4.31
) and (
4.32
) into (
4.30
), we obtain

(4.33)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\sqrt{\frac{x}{t^{3/2}}}\leq\varrho_{t}(E_{t}-x)=\frac{{\rm Im}[w]}{t\pi}\leq\frac{2}{\pi}\sqrt{\frac{x}{t^{3/2}}}.

This finishes the proof of (
4.4
).
∎

Proof of
Item

4
in
4.4
.

To prove (
4.6
), we first show that
for
-\sqrt{t}\leq x\leq\sqrt{t}
, we have

(4.34)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4\pi}\sqrt{\frac{E^{+}_{t}-x}{t^{3/2}}}\leq\varrho_{t}(x)\leq\frac{4}{\pi}\sqrt{\frac{E^{+}_{t}-x}{t^{3/2}}},

and for
\sqrt{t}\leq x\leq E^{+}_{t}
we have slightly better estimate

(4.35)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\sqrt{\frac{E^{+}_{t}-x}{t^{3/2}}}\leq\varrho_{t}(x)\leq\frac{2}{\pi}\sqrt{\frac{E^{+}_{t}-x}{t^{3/2}}}.

The same statement holds close to
E_{t}^{-}
, i.e., for
E_{t}^{-}\leq x\leq-\sqrt{t}

(4.36)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\sqrt{\frac{x-E^{-}_{t}}{t^{3/2}}}\leq\varrho_{t}(x)\leq\frac{2}{\pi}\sqrt{\frac{x-E^{-}_{t}}{t^{3/2}}}.

The proofs of (
4.34
), (
4.35
) and (
4.36
) are very similar, so we will only prove (
4.35
). The statement (
4.35
) for
2\sqrt{t}\sqrt{1-(2M)^{-2}}\leq x\leq E_{t}^{+}
follows from (
4.4
). In the case when
\sqrt{t}\leq x\leq 2\sqrt{t}\sqrt{1-(2M)^{-2}}\leq 2\sqrt{t}-\sqrt{t}/4M^{2}
, we have

(4.37)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sqrt{\frac{t^{-3/2}(E^{+}_{t}-x)}{(2t)^{-2}(4t-x^{2})}}&\leq\sqrt{\frac{4\sqrt{t}(2\sqrt{t}+2c-x)}{(2\sqrt{t}-x)(2\sqrt{t}+x)}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{4\sqrt{t}(\sqrt{t}/4M^{2}+2c)}{(\sqrt{t}/4M^{2})3\sqrt{t}}}\\
&\leq\sqrt{\frac{4\sqrt{t}(\sqrt{t}/4M^{2}+2\sqrt{t}/(25M^{2}))}{(\sqrt{t}/4M^{2})3\sqrt{t}}}=\sqrt{\frac{44}{25}}\leq\frac{4}{3},\end{split}

where in the first statement we used that
|E_{t}^{+}-2\sqrt{t}|\leq 2c
from the first statement of the lemma; in the second statement we used that
\sqrt{t}\leq x\leq 2\sqrt{t}-\sqrt{t}/4M^{2}
so
2\sqrt{t}+x\geq 3\sqrt{t}
, and
(2\sqrt{t}+2c-x)/(2\sqrt{t}-x)\leq(\sqrt{t}/4M^{2}+2c)/(\sqrt{t}/4M^{2})
; and in the third statement we used
c\leq\sqrt{t}/(25M^{2})
from the assumption of
4.4
. Moreover,

(4.38)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sqrt{\frac{t^{-3/2}(E^{+}_{t}-x)}{(2t)^{-2}(4t-x^{2})}}&=\sqrt{\displaystyle\frac{4t^{1/2}(E_{t}^{+}-x)}{(2\sqrt{t}-x)(2\sqrt{t}+x)}}\geq\sqrt{\frac{2\sqrt{t}-2c-x}{2\sqrt{t}-x}}\\
&\geq\sqrt{\frac{\sqrt{t}/4M^{2}-2c}{\sqrt{t}/4M^{2}}}\geq\sqrt{\frac{\sqrt{t}/4M^{2}-2\sqrt{t}/(25M^{2})}{\sqrt{t}/4M^{2}}}=\sqrt{\frac{17}{25}}\geq\frac{3}{4},\end{split}

where the first statement follows from factoring
4t-x^{2}
; the second statement follows from
|E_{t}^{+}-2\sqrt{t}|\leq 2c
from the first statement of the lemma and
x\leq 2\sqrt{t}-\sqrt{t}/4M^{2}\leq 2\sqrt{t}-2c
; the third statement follows from
x\leq 2\sqrt{t}-\sqrt{t}/4M^{2}
so
(2\sqrt{t}-2c-x)/(2\sqrt{t}-x)\geq(\sqrt{t}/4M^{2}-2c)/(\sqrt{t}/4M^{2})
; and the fourth statement follows from
c\leq\sqrt{t}/(25M^{2})
from the assumption of
4.4
. Then (
4.35
) follows from (
4.3
) by combining (
4.37
) and (
4.38
),

(4.39)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\sqrt{\frac{E_{t}^{+}-x}{t^{3/2}}}\leq\frac{2}{3}\frac{\sqrt{4t-x^{2}}}{2\pi t}\leq\varrho_{t}(x)\leq\frac{3}{2}\frac{\sqrt{4t-x^{2}}}{2\pi t}\leq\frac{2}{\pi}\sqrt{\frac{E_{t}^{+}-x}{t^{3/2}}}.

Now, recall that
\gamma_{t}(y)
is the inverted cumulative density of
\varrho_{t}
(as defined in (
2.13
)), and so
-\del_{t}\gamma_{t}(y)=1/\varrho_{t}(\gamma_{t}(y))
(where we have used the facts by (
4.34
), (
4.35
), and (
4.36
) that
\varrho_{t}(y)>0
for each
y\in[E_{t}^{-},E_{t}^{+}]
). Next we show that
\gamma_{t}(4/7)\geq-\sqrt{t}
. This follows from that

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{-\sqrt{t}}\varrho_{t}(x)dx\leq\displaystyle\int_{E_{t}^{-}}^{-\sqrt{t}}\frac{2}{\pi}\sqrt{\frac{x-E_{t}^{-}}{t^{3/2}}}dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\pi}\frac{2}{3}\frac{(\sqrt{t}+2c)^{3/2}}{t^{3/4}}

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{3\pi}\left(1+\frac{2c}{\sqrt{t}}\right)^{3/2}\leq\frac{4}{3\pi}\left(1+\frac{4}{25M^{2}}\right)^{3/2}\leq\frac{3}{7},

where in the first inequality we used (
4.36
); in the second inequality we used
E_{t}^{-}\geq-2\sqrt{t}-2c
from the first statement of the lemma; and in the last two inequalities we used that
c/\sqrt{t}\leq 2/25M^{2}
and
M=100
following from our assumption in
4.4
. We conclude from combining (
4.34
) and (
4.35
) that, for
\gamma_{t}(4/7)\leq x\leq E_{t}^{+}
,

(4.40)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4\pi}\sqrt{\frac{E_{t}^{+}-x}{t^{3/2}}}\leq\varrho_{t}(x)\leq\frac{4}{\pi}\sqrt{\frac{E_{t}^{+}-x}{t^{3/2}}}.

By plugging
x=\gamma_{t}(y)
with
0\leq y\leq 4/7
into (
4.40
), we get

(4.41)

\displaystyle\frac{\pi}{4}\sqrt{\frac{t^{3/2}}{E_{t}^{+}-\gamma_{t}(y)}}\leq-\del_{y}\gamma_{t}(y)=-\frac{1}{\varrho_{t}(\gamma_{t}(y))}\leq 4\pi\sqrt{\frac{t^{3/2}}{E_{t}^{+}-\gamma_{t}(y)}}.

We can rearrange the above expression as

(4.42)

\displaystyle\frac{3\pi}{8}t^{3/4}\leq\del_{y}(E_{t}^{+}-\gamma_{t}(y))^{3/2}\leq 6\pi t^{3/4}.

Integrating from
0
to
y
, we conclude that

(4.43)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{3\pi y}{8}\right)^{2/3}\sqrt{t}\leq E_{t}^{+}-\gamma_{t}(y).

By plugging (
4.43
) into (
4.41
), we get

(4.44)

\displaystyle-\del_{y}\gamma_{t}(y)=-\frac{1}{\varrho_{t}(\gamma_{t}(y))}\leq 4\pi\sqrt{\frac{t^{3/2}}{E_{t}^{+}-\gamma_{t}(y)}}\leq 8\pi(3\pi y)^{-1/3}\sqrt{t}.

By integrating the above upper bound from
y
to
y^{\prime}
, we get the upper bound

\displaystyle\gamma_{t}(y)-\gamma_{t}(y^{\prime})\leq\int_{y}^{y^{\prime}}8\pi(3\pi u)^{-1/3}\sqrt{t}{\rm d}u=(24\pi)^{2/3}t^{1/2}(y^{\prime 2/3}-y^{2/3}).

This finishes the proof of (
4.6
).
∎

Proof of
4.2
.

Recall from
2.9
that, with probability
1-Ce^{-(\log n)^{2}}
, we have for any
0\leq t\leq{\mathsf{T}}
and
1\leq i\leq n
that

(4.45)

\displaystyle\gamma_{i+\lfloor(\log n)^{5}\rfloor}(t)-n^{-1}\leq\lambda_{i}(t)\leq\gamma_{i-\lfloor(\log n)^{5}\rfloor}(t)+n^{-1}.

We will only prove
4.2
for
i\leq n/2
, as the case
i\geq n/2
follows by the same argument. We notice that
t\geq{\mathfrak{B}}\sqrt{c}=2^{60}{\mathsf{T}}^{18}\sqrt{c}\geq 10^{18}c^{2}\geq(25c\cdot 100^{2})^{2}
, where we used
{\mathsf{T}}\geq 1
and
c\leq 1
. This verifies the assumption in
4.4
. Thus for
1\leq i<j\leq 4n/7
, using (
4.6
) we have

(4.46)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\gamma_{i}(t)-\gamma_{j}(t)&\leq(24\pi)^{2/3}t^{1/2}((j-1/2)^{2/3}-(i-1/2)^{2/3})n^{-2/3}\\
&\leq(24\pi)^{2/3}t^{1/2}(j^{2/3}-i^{2/3})n^{-2/3}+\frac{\log n}{i^{1/3}n^{2/3}},\end{split}

provided
n
is large enough. Then, (
4.45
) and (
4.46
) together imply that with probability
1-Ce^{-(\log n)^{2}}
we have for each
2(\log n)^{5}\leq i\leq n/2
that

(4.47)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}|\lambda_{i}(t)-\gamma_{i}(t)|\leq|\gamma_{i-\lfloor(\log n)^{5}\rfloor}(t)-\gamma_{i+\lfloor(\log n)^{5}\rfloor}(t)|+2n^{-1}\\
&\leq(24\pi)^{2/3}t^{1/2}n^{-2/3}((i+\lfloor(\log n)^{5}\rfloor)^{2/3}-(i-\lfloor(\log n)^{5}\rfloor)^{2/3})+\frac{\log n}{i^{1/3}n^{2/3}}+2n^{-1}\\
&\leq(24\pi)^{2/3}t^{1/2}n^{-2/3}\frac{2\lfloor(\log n)^{5}\rfloor}{(i-\lfloor(\log n)^{5}\rfloor)^{1/3}}+\frac{\log n}{i^{1/3}n^{2/3}}+2n^{-1}\leq\frac{(\log n)^{6}}{i^{1/3}n^{2/3}},\end{split}

provided
n
is large enough.

To address the remaining
i
, observe from
1.3
and (
4.45
) that, with probability
1-Ce^{-(\log n)^{2}}
, we have for each
1\leq i\leq 2(\log n)^{5}
that

(4.48)

\displaystyle\gamma_{i+\lfloor(\log n)^{5}\rfloor}(t)\leq\lambda_{i}(t)\leq\lambda_{1}(t)\leq E_{t}^{+}+\frac{(\log n)^{15}}{n^{2/3}},\quad\text{so }\quad\lambda_{i}(t),\gamma_{i}(t)\in\left[\gamma_{3\lfloor(\log n)^{5}\rfloor}(t),E_{t}^{+}+\frac{(\log n)^{15}}{n^{2/3}}\right].

It follows that for each
1\leq i\leq 2(\log n)^{5}
, and
n
large enough

(4.49)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\lambda_{i}(t)-\gamma_{i}(t)|&\leq\frac{(\log n)^{15}}{n^{1/3}}+|E_{t}^{+}-\gamma_{3\lfloor(\log n)^{5}\rfloor}(t)|\\
&\leq\frac{(\log n)^{15}}{n^{1/3}}+(24\pi)^{2/3}t^{1/2}n^{-2/3}(3(\log n)^{5})^{2/3}\leq\frac{(\log n)^{17}}{i^{1/3}n^{2/3}},\end{split}

where the first inequality follows from (
4.48
) and the second from (
4.6
).
The two estimates (
4.47
) and (
4.49
) together give
4.2
.
∎

Proof of
4.3
.

4.3
is a quick consequence of
4.2
. We rescale time by
n^{1/3}
and space by
n^{2/3}
, and shift
x_{1}(0)
to
c/2
, defining

(4.50)

\displaystyle\lambda_{i}(t)=\frac{x_{i}(tn^{1/3})-x_{1}(0)+cn^{2/3}/2}{n^{2/3}},

Then, it is quickly verified that
\{\lambda_{i}(t)\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
is a Dyson Brownian motion satisfying (
1.2
); we also have
\lambda_{i}(0)\in[-c/2,c/2]
for each
1\leq i\leq n
. Moreover, by taking
{\mathsf{T}}=B
, the assumptions in
4.2
are satisfied; hence, with probability
1-\mathcal{O}(e^{-(\log n)^{2}})
, (
4.1
) holds for each
t\in[1/B,B]
and
1\leq i\leq n
.

This, together with (
4.6
) gives that, with probability
1-Ce^{-(\log n)^{2}}
, for each
t\in[1/B,B]
and
1\leq i<j\leq n/2
we have

(4.51)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\lambda_{i}(t)-\lambda_{j}(t)|&\leq|\gamma_{i}(t)-\gamma_{j}(t)|+|\lambda_{i}(t)-\gamma_{i}(t)|+|\lambda_{j}(t)-\gamma_{j}(t)|\\
&\leq(24\pi)^{2/3}t^{1/2}\frac{(j-1/2)^{2/3}-(i-1/2)^{2/3}}{n^{2/3}}+\frac{2(\log n)^{17}}{i^{1/3}n^{2/3}}\\
&\leq(24\pi)^{2/3}t^{1/2}\frac{j^{2/3}-i^{2/3}}{n^{2/3}}+\frac{(\log n)^{20}}{i^{1/3}n^{2/3}}.\end{split}

4.3
follows from substituting (
4.50
) into (
4.51
).
∎

4.2.
Stronger Concentration Bounds for Short Time

For very short time
t\ll 1
, the concentration of the largest particle on the scale
n^{-2/3+\oo(1)}
provided by
1.3
may not be optimal. In this section, we study this phenomenon through the example when the initial data
\mu_{0}^{(n)}
is prescribed by the classical locations of the uniform measure
\textbf{1}_{[-1,0]}\mathrm{d}x
. Throughout this section, we set
\mu_{0}=\textbf{1}_{[-1,0]}\mathrm{d}x
, and we denote by
\mu_{t}=\mu_{0}\boxplus\mu_{\semci}^{(t)}(x)
the free convolution of
\mu_{0}
with the rescaled semicircle distribution
\mu_{\semci}^{(t)}
. Let
\varrho_{t}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
denote the density of
\mu_{t}
with respect to Lebesgue measure. Also let
E_{t}^{-}=\min(\supp\mu_{t})
and
E_{t}^{+}=\max(\supp\mu_{t})
.

The following lemma approximates
E_{t}^{+}
and
\varrho_{t}
(with its derivative) around
E_{t}^{+}
.

Lemma 4.5
.

For any real number
{\mathsf{T}}\geq 1
, there exists a constant
C=C({\mathsf{T}})>1
such that, for any
0\leq t\leq{\mathsf{T}}
, the following statements hold.

(1)

The density
\varrho_{t}(y)
is symmetric around
-1/2
, and satisfies

(4.52)

\displaystyle\varrho_{t}(y)\leq 1,\qquad\text{for each $y\in\mathbb{R}$}.

The spectral edge satisfies

(4.53)

\displaystyle|E_{t}^{+}-(1+\log(t^{-1}))t|\leq Ct^{2},\quad|E_{t}^{-}-(-1-(1+\log(t^{-1}))t)|\leq Ct^{2},

(2)

The density is bounded:

(4.54)

\displaystyle C^{-1}\min\left\{\sqrt{\frac{x}{t}},1\right\}\leq\varrho_{t}(E^{+}_{t}-x)\leq C\min\left\{\sqrt{\frac{x}{t}},1\right\},\qquad\text{whenever $-1/2\leq E_{t}^{+}-x\leq E_{t}^{+}$.}

and

(4.55)

\displaystyle C^{-1}\min\left\{\sqrt{\frac{x}{t}},1\right\}\leq\varrho_{t}(E^{-}_{t}+x)\leq C\min\left\{\sqrt{\frac{x}{t}},1\right\},\qquad\text{whenever $E_{t}^{-}\leq E_{t}^{-}+x\leq-1/2$.}

(3)

The derivative of the density satisfies:

(4.56)

\displaystyle|\varrho^{\prime}_{t}(E^{+}_{t}-x)|\leq C\min\left\{\sqrt{\frac{1}{xt}},\frac{t}{x^{2}}\right\},\qquad\text{whenever $-1/2\leq E_{t}^{+}-x\leq E_{t}^{+}$.}

and

(4.57)

\displaystyle|\varrho^{\prime}_{t}(E^{-}_{t}+x)|\leq C\min\left\{\sqrt{\frac{1}{xt}},\frac{t}{x^{2}}\right\},\qquad\text{whenever $E_{t}^{-}\leq E_{t}^{-}+x\leq-1/2$.}

Proof of
4.5
.

We denote the Stieltjes transform of
\varrho_{t}
by

\displaystyle m_{t}(z)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\varrho_{t}(x)dx}{x-z},\qquad\text{so that}\qquad m_{0}(z)=\log\frac{z}{z+1}.

Recall from (
2.8
) that
m_{t}
satisfies

(4.58)

\displaystyle m_{t}(z_{t})=m_{0}(z),\qquad\text{where}\quad z_{t}=z_{t}(z)=z-tm_{0}(z).

Figure 4.
Left panel shows
\del\Lambda_{t}\cap\mathbb{H}
from
-1-\xi(t)
to
\xi(t)
, and the density
\mu_{0}
(the gray region).
Right panel is the density
\mu_{t}=\mu_{0}\boxplus\mu_{\semci}^{(t)}(x)
supported on
[E_{t}^{-},E_{t}^{+}]
. Both of them are symmetric around
-1/2
.

Recalling the region
\Lambda_{t}
from (
2.7
) and the function
v_{t}
from (
2.9
),
Item

1
in
2.3
indicates that we can parametrize
\del\Lambda_{t}
as
\{u+\mathrm{i}v_{t}(u):u\in{\mathbb{R}}\}
; we also have

(4.59)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t}\geq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{{\rm d}\mu_{0}(x)}{|x-(u+\mathrm{i}v_{t}(u))|^{2}}=\int_{-1}^{0}\frac{{\rm d}x}{(x-u)^{2}+v_{t}(u)^{2}}.

From
Item

5
in
2.3
, there exists
\xi^{+}(t)
and
\xi^{-}(t)
such that
\xi^{\pm}(t)-tm_{0}(\xi^{\pm}(t))=E^{\pm}_{t}
and
\xi^{\pm}(t)
are characterized by

(4.60)

\displaystyle m_{0}^{\prime}(\xi^{\pm}(t))=\frac{1}{t},\qquad\text{where we observe that}\qquad m_{0}^{\prime}(z)=\int_{-1}^{0}\frac{{\rm d}x}{(x-z)^{2}}=\frac{1}{z}-\frac{1}{z+1}.

By the symmetry for
\mu_{0}
around
-1/2
, we have
\xi^{-}(t)=-1-\xi^{+}(t)
,
E_{t}^{-}=-E_{t}^{+}-1
, and
\varrho_{t}(E_{t}^{+}-x)=\varrho_{t}(E_{t}^{-}+x)
; see
Figure

4
. Hence, it suffices to show all of the bounds stated in the lemma for
E_{t}^{+}
(or for
x
close to
E_{t}^{+}
). So, throughout this proof, we abbreviate
E_{t}=E_{t}^{+}
, and
\xi(t)=\xi^{+}(t)
.

From
2.2
that, for any
y\in{\mathbb{R}}
, there exists
w=w_{t}(u)=u+\mathrm{i}v_{t}(u)\in\del\Lambda_{t}
such that
y=w-tm_{0}(w)
. Next we show that
v_{t}(u)>0
for
-1-\xi(t)<u<\xi(t)
as in
Figure

4
. Otherwise,
v_{t}(u)=0
; we may assume by symmetry that
u\geq-1/2
. If
u\leq 0
, then we have from (
4.59
) that

(4.61)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t}\geq\int_{-1}^{0}\frac{{\rm d}x}{(x-u)^{2}}=\infty,

which is a contradiction. If
0<u<\xi(t)
, then (
4.59
) gives

(4.62)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t}\geq\int_{-1}^{0}\frac{{\rm d}x}{(x-u)^{2}}>\int_{-1}^{0}\frac{{\rm d}x}{(x-\xi(t))^{2}}=\frac{1}{t},

where in the middle inequality we used that for
x\in[-1,0]
we have
\xi(t)-x>u-x>0
. This is again a contradiction. Thus
v_{t}(u)>0
for
-1-\xi(t)<u<\xi(t)
.
From
Item

4
in
2.3
,
y\in{\mathbb{R}}
satisfies
\varrho_{t}(y)>0
if and only if
w\in\del\Lambda_{t}\cap\mathbb{H}
. Therefore, the discussion above, together with
Item

1
and
Item

4
in
2.3
gives

(4.63)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{{\rm d}\mu_{0}(x)}{|x-(u+\mathrm{i}v_{t}(u))|^{2}},\quad\varrho_{t}(y)=\frac{{\rm Im}[w]}{\pi t}=\frac{v_{t}(u)}{\pi t},\quad\text{for}\quad y\in[-1-E_{t},E_{t}].

Using the relation (
4.60
), we can solve
\xi(t)
explicitly, for each
0\leq t\leq{\mathsf{T}}
. In particular, we have

(4.64)

\displaystyle\xi(t)^{2}+\xi(t)=t,\quad\text{so}\quad\frac{t}{2{\mathsf{T}}}\leq\xi(t)=\frac{2t}{1+\sqrt{1+4t}}\leq t,\quad\text{which implies}\quad|\xi(t)-t|\leq\big|\xi(t)\big|^{2}\leq t^{2}.

By (
4.63
), we have

(4.65)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{{\rm d}\mu_{0}(x)}{|x-w|^{2}}\leq\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{{\rm d}x}{(x-u)^{2}+v_{t}(u)^{2}}=\frac{\pi}{v_{t}(u)},

and hence
\varrho_{t}(y)=v_{t}(u)/\pi t\leq 1
, verifying (
4.52
). Moreover, using the estimate of
\xi(t)
from (
4.64
), with the relation
\xi(t)-tm_{0}(\xi(t))=E_{t}
from
Item

5
in
2.3
, we have

\displaystyle E_{t}=\xi(t)-tm_{0}(\xi(t))=\xi(t)-t\log\frac{\xi(t)}{\xi(t)+1}=(1+\log(t^{-1})+\OO(t))t.

This finishes the proof of (
4.53
).

Next we show (
4.54
), from which (
4.55
) would follow by symmetry. From
Item

4
in
2.3
,
\varrho_{t}(E_{t}-x)>0
if and only if there exists some
w\in\partial\Lambda_{t}\cap\mathbb{H}
such that
E_{t}-x=w-tm_{0}(w)
; in this case,
\varrho_{t}(E_{t}-x)=(t\pi)^{-1}\Imaginary w
. Equivalently, let
\Delta=w-\xi(t)
. We have
\varrho_{t}(E_{t}-x)>0
if and only if there exists some
\Delta=\Delta(x)
satisfying
\Delta+\xi(t)\in\mathbb{H}
such that

(4.66)

\displaystyle\xi(t)+\Delta-tm_{0}(\xi(t)+\Delta)=E_{t}-x,\quad\text{in which case}\quad\varrho_{t}(E_{t}-x)={\rm Im}[\Delta]/t\pi.

In the following we fix the constant
M=(4{\mathsf{T}})^{3}
and let
w=\xi(t)+\Delta
be some complex number. If
|\Delta|\leq t/M
then by a Taylor expansion around
\xi(t)
, using (
4.60
), we get

(4.67)

\displaystyle m_{0}(w)-m_{0}(\xi(t))=m^{\prime}_{0}(\xi(t))\Delta+\frac{m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))\Delta^{2}}{2}+\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(w)\Delta^{3}}{6}=\frac{\Delta}{t}+\frac{m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))\Delta^{2}}{2}+\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(w)\Delta^{3}}{6}.

where

(4.68)

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{E}}(w)|\leq 2\max_{|z-\xi(t)|\leq t/M}\left|\frac{1}{z^{3}}-\frac{1}{(z+1)^{3}}\right|\leq\frac{(4{\mathsf{T}})^{3}}{t^{3}},

where we used the facts that
m_{0}(z)=\log z-\log(z+1)
(so that
m_{0}^{(3)}(z)=2z^{-3}-2(z+1)^{-3}
) and (
4.64
), which implies that for
|z-\xi(t)|\leq t/M
we have
|z|,|z+1|\geq t/(2{\mathsf{T}})-t/M\geq t/(16^{1/3}{\mathsf{T}})
.

By plugging (
4.67
) into (
4.66
) (and using the fact that
E_{t}=\xi(t)-tm_{0}(\xi(t))
), we get that
\varrho_{t}(E_{t}-x)>0
if and only if there exists some
\Delta=\Delta(x)\in\mathbb{H}
such that

(4.69)

\displaystyle\frac{x}{t}=\frac{m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))\Delta^{2}}{2}+\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(w)\Delta^{3}}{6}=\frac{m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))\Delta^{2}}{2}\left(1+\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(w)\Delta}{3m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))}\right).

By explicit computation using the formula of
m_{0}(z)=\log z-\log(z+1)
(so that
m_{0}^{(2)}(z)=(z+1)^{-2}-z^{-2}
and
m_{0}^{(3)}(z)=2z^{-3}-2(z+1)^{-3}
) and
\xi(t)\leq t\leq{\mathsf{T}}
from (
4.64
), we have

(4.70)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{1}{t^{2}}\leq-m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))=\frac{2\xi(t)+1}{\xi(t)^{2}(\xi(t)+1)^{2}}=\frac{1+2\xi(t)}{t^{2}}\leq\frac{3{\mathsf{T}}}{t^{2}},\\
&|m_{0}^{(3)}(\xi(t))|=2\left|\frac{3\xi(t)^{2}+3\xi(t)+1}{\xi(t)^{3}(\xi(t)+1)^{3}}\right|=2\left|\frac{3t+1}{t^{3}}\right|\leq\frac{8{\mathsf{T}}}{t^{3}}.\end{split}

It follows that the error term on righthand side of (
4.69
), when
|\Delta|\leq t/M
, is bounded by

(4.71)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(w)\Delta}{3m_{0}^{(2)}\big(\xi(t)\big)}\right|\leq\frac{(4{\mathsf{T}})^{3}}{t^{3}}\frac{t}{M}\frac{t^{2}}{3}\leq\frac{1}{3},

where we used (
4.68
) and (
4.70
) (and the fact that
M=(4\mathsf{T})^{3}
).

Next we show the righthand side of (
4.69
) as a function of
\Delta
is a surjection from
|\Delta|\leq t/M
onto
\{u\in{\mathbb{C}}:|u|\leq 1/4M^{2}\}
. Fix any
|u|\leq 1/4M^{2}
; we will use Rouché’s theorem to compare the two holomorphic functions

(4.72)

\displaystyle\frac{m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))\Delta^{2}}{2}\left(1+\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(w)\Delta}{3m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))}\right)-u,\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))\Delta^{2}}{2}-u

on the domain
\{\Delta\in{\mathbb{C}}:|\Delta|\leq t/M\}
. Using the first statement in (
4.70
), we notice that
m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))\Delta^{2}/2-u
has a root with
|\Delta|\leq t/M
. On the boundary, for any
\Delta\in{\mathbb{C}}
such that
|\Delta|=t/M
, we can upper bound the difference

(4.73)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\left|\left(\frac{m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))\Delta^{2}}{2}\left(1+\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(w)\Delta}{3m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))}\right)-u\right)-\left(\frac{m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))\Delta^{2}}{2}-u\right)\right|\\
&=\left|\frac{m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))\Delta^{2}}{2}\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(w)\Delta}{3m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))}\right|\leq\frac{1}{3}\left|\frac{m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))\Delta^{2}}{2}\right|\leq\left|\frac{m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))\Delta^{2}}{2}-u\right|\end{split}

where in the second statement we used (
4.71
); in the last inequality we used that, when
|\Delta|=t/M
,

(4.74)

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{3}\frac{m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))|\Delta|^{2}}{2}=-\frac{2}{3}\frac{m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))}{2}\frac{t^{2}}{M^{2}}>\frac{1}{4M^{2}}\geq|u|,

where the second statement follows from
-m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))\geq 1/t^{2}
from the first statement in (
4.70
).
The relation (
4.73
) verifies the conditions of Rouché’s theorem, and we conclude that the first function in (
4.72
) (as a function of
\Delta
) also has a root with
|\Delta|\leq t/M
. Thus we can use (
4.70
) to solve for
\Delta=\Delta(x)
for any
0\leq x\leq t/4M^{2}
. We collect it here for the future use:

(4.75)

\displaystyle|\Delta(x)|\leq t/M,\quad\text{when},\quad 0\leq x\leq t/4M^{2}

By (
4.69
), we can solve for
\Delta
, given by

(4.76)

\displaystyle\Delta=\mathrm{i}\sqrt{\frac{2x}{-tm_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))}}\left(1+\frac{{\mathcal{E}}(w)\Delta}{3m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))}\right)^{-1/2}.

By plugging (
4.70
) and (
4.71
) into (
4.76
), we conclude for
0\leq x\leq t/4M^{2}
that

(4.77)

\displaystyle\sqrt{\displaystyle\frac{tx}{2\mathsf{T}}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{2x}{(3{\mathsf{T}}/t)}}\left(1+\frac{1}{3}\right)^{-1/2}\leq|\Delta|,{\rm Im}[\Delta]\leq\sqrt{\frac{2x}{(1/t)}}\left(1-\frac{1}{3}\right)^{-1/2}\leq\sqrt{3tx}.

As a consequence, using (
4.63
) and (
4.77
), we get

(4.78)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi}\sqrt{\displaystyle\frac{x}{2t\mathsf{T}}}\leq\varrho(E_{t}-x)=\frac{{\rm Im}[\Delta]}{t\pi}\leq\frac{1}{\pi}\sqrt{\frac{3x}{t}}

for
0\leq x\leq t/4M^{2}
. For
x\geq t/4M^{2}
, next we show

\varrho_{t}(E_{t}-x)
is an increasing function of
x
for
0\leq x\leq E_{t}+1/2
. Then this, together with (
4.52
) and the second statement in (
4.77
), would imply that

(4.79)

\displaystyle 1\geq\varrho_{t}(E_{t}-x)\geq\varrho_{t}(E_{t}-t/4M^{2})\geq\frac{1}{2\pi M\sqrt{2{\mathsf{T}}}},\quad\text{for}\quad t/4M^{2}\leq x\leq E_{t}+1/2,

so (
4.78
) and (
4.79
) together give (
4.54
).

Next we use (
4.63
) to show that

\varrho_{t}(E_{t}-x)
is an increasing function of
x
for
0\leq x\leq E_{t}+1/2
. First we notice that on
[-1/2,\xi(t)]
,
v_{t}(u)
is a decreasing function. Otherwise, there are some
-1/2\leq u<u^{\prime}\leq\xi(t)
such that
v_{t}(u)\leq v_{t}(u^{\prime})
. Then (
4.63
) leads to the contradiction,

(4.80)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t}=\int_{-1}^{0}\frac{{\rm d}x}{(x-u)^{2}+v_{t}(u)^{2}}\geq\int_{-1}^{0}\frac{{\rm d}x}{(x-u)^{2}+v_{t}(u^{\prime})^{2}}>\int_{-1}^{0}\frac{{\rm d}x}{(x-u^{\prime})^{2}+v_{t}(u^{\prime})^{2}}=\frac{1}{t},

where the third statement follows from the following bound (by taking
b=v_{t}(u^{\prime})
and integrating
a
from
u
to
u^{\prime}
): for any
a>-1/2
and
b\geq 0
,

(4.81)

\displaystyle\del_{a}\int_{-1}^{0}\frac{{\rm d}x}{(x-a)^{2}+b^{2}}=-\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{0}\partial_{x}\bigg(\displaystyle\frac{1}{(x-a)^{2}+b^{2}}\bigg)\mathrm{d}x=\displaystyle\frac{1}{(a+1)^{2}+b^{2}}-\displaystyle\frac{1}{a^{2}+b^{2}}<0.

From the discussion above (
4.63
), and
Item

3
and
Item

4
from
2.3
, the map
M
(from (
2.7
)) maps
\{u+v_{t}(u)\mathrm{i}:-1-\xi(t)\leq u\leq\xi(t)\}
increasingly (as a function of
u
) to
\supp\mu_{t}=[-1-E_{t},E_{t}]
(see
Figure

4
).
By symmetry around
-1/2
,

(4.82)

\displaystyle M\text{ maps }\{u+v_{t}(u)\mathrm{i}:-1/2\leq u\leq\xi(t)\}\text{ increasingly (as a function of $u$) to }[-1/2,E_{t}].

This together with
\varrho_{t}(M(u+v_{t}(u)\mathrm{i})=v_{t}(u)/t\pi
from (
4.63
), and
v_{t}(u)
is decreasing on
[-1/2,\xi(t)]
, leads to that
\varrho_{t}(y)
is decreasing on
[-1/2,E_{t}]
. Thus
\varrho_{t}(E_{t}-x)
is an increasing function of
x
for
0\leq x\leq E_{t}+1/2
, and this together with (
4.79
) finishes the claim.

We can further estimate the derivative of the density. As mentioned previously, we can view the quantity
\Delta
in (
4.66
) as a function of
x
. Taking its derivative with respect to
x
yields

(4.83)

\displaystyle\del_{x}\Delta=-\frac{1}{1-tm_{0}^{\prime}(\xi(t)+\Delta)},\quad\varrho(E_{t}-x)={\rm Im}[\Delta]/t\pi.

Recall
M=(4{\mathsf{T}})^{3}
. We distinguish between whether
x\leq t/(4M^{2})
or
x\geq t/(4M^{2})
.
If
x\leq t/(4M^{2})
, recall from (
4.75
), we have
|\Delta|\leq t/M
. In this case, the Taylor expansion of
m_{0}^{\prime}(\xi(t)+\Delta)
around
\xi(t)
is

(4.84)

\displaystyle m_{0}^{\prime}(\xi(t)+\Delta)=m_{0}^{\prime}(\xi(t))+m_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))\Delta+\frac{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(\Delta)\Delta^{2}}{2},\quad|\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(\Delta)|\leq\frac{(4{\mathsf{T}})^{3}}{t^{3}},

where the bound of the remainder term follows from (
4.68
). This together with
m^{\prime}_{0}(\xi(t))=1/t
from (
4.60
) gives

(4.85)

\displaystyle\left|1-tm_{0}^{\prime}(\xi(t)+\Delta)+tm_{0}^{(2)}(\xi(t))\Delta\right|=\frac{|t\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}(w)\Delta^{2}|}{2}\leq\frac{2^{5}{\mathsf{T}}^{3}\Delta^{2}}{t^{2}}.

We can reformulate (
4.85
) as

\displaystyle\left|1-tm_{0}^{\prime}(\xi(t)+\Delta)\right|\geq\frac{|\Delta|}{t}-\frac{2^{5}{\mathsf{T}}^{3}\Delta^{2}}{t^{2}}\geq\frac{|\Delta|}{2t}\geq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2{\mathsf{T}}}}\sqrt{\frac{x}{t}},

where the first equality follows from (
4.85
) and the first statement of (
4.70
);
the second inequality is from
|\Delta|\leq M^{-1}t
and
M=(4\mathsf{T})^{3}
; and the last inequality follows from the first statement in (
4.77
) and our assumption
x\leq t/(4M^{2})
.
Plugging the above estimate into (
4.83
), we conclude that

(4.86)

\displaystyle|\varrho_{t}^{\prime}(E_{t}-x)|\leq\frac{|\del_{x}\Delta|}{t\pi}=\frac{1}{t\pi}\frac{1}{|1-tm_{0}^{\prime}(\xi(t)+\Delta)|}\leq\frac{\sqrt{8{\mathsf{T}}}}{\pi\sqrt{xt}}.

Now assume that
x\geq t/4M^{2}
. Recalling that
w=\xi(t)+\Delta=u+v_{t}\mathrm{i}
, we must upper bound

(4.87)

\displaystyle\varrho_{t}^{\prime}(E_{t}-x)=\frac{\del_{x}{\rm Im}[\Delta]}{t\pi}=-\frac{{\rm Im}[m_{0}^{\prime}(\xi(t)+\Delta)]}{\pi|1-tm_{0}^{\prime}(\xi(t)+\Delta)|^{2}},

where the second statement follows from taking imaginary part of the first statement in (
4.83
). By (
4.82
) and (
4.65
), we only need to upper bound (
4.87
) for

(4.88)

\displaystyle-1/2\leq{\rm Re}[w]=u\leq\xi(t),\quad{\rm Im}[w]\leq\pi t.

For the denominator in (
4.87
) observe that from the definition (
2.1
) that

(4.89)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\phantom{{}={}}{\rm Re}[1-tm_{0}^{\prime}(w)]=1-t{\rm Re}\left[\int_{-1}^{0}\frac{{\rm d}x}{(x-w)^{2}}\right]=1-t\int_{-1}^{0}\frac{{\rm Re}[(x-\overline{w})^{2}]{\rm d}x}{|x-w|^{4}}\\
&=1-t\int_{-1}^{0}\frac{(|w-x|^{2}-2{\rm Im}[w]^{2}){\rm d}x}{|w-x|^{4}}=1-t\int_{-1}^{0}\frac{{\rm d}x}{|w-x|^{2}}+2t{\rm Im}[w]^{2}\int_{-1}^{0}\frac{{\rm d}x}{|w-x|^{4}}\\
&=2t{\rm Im}[w]^{2}\int_{-1}^{0}\frac{{\rm d}x}{|w-x|^{4}}\geq 2t\left(\frac{t}{2M\sqrt{\mathsf{2T}}}\right)^{2}\int_{-1}^{0}\frac{{\rm d}x}{|u+\mathrm{i}\pi t-x|^{4}}=\frac{1}{4M^{2}{\mathsf{T}}}t^{3}\int_{-1}^{0}\frac{{\rm d}x}{((u-x)^{2}+(\pi t)^{2})^{2}}\end{aligned}

where for the first equality in the third line we used (
4.63
); for the middle inequality we used that
{\rm Im}[w]={\rm Im}[\Delta(x)]=t\pi\varrho_{t}(E_{t}-x)
from (
4.66
), and
\varrho_{t}(E_{t}-x)\geq 1/(2\pi M\sqrt{2{\mathsf{T}}})
for
x\geq t/4M^{2}
from (
4.79
),
thus

(4.90)

\displaystyle{\rm Im}[w]\geq\frac{t}{2M\sqrt{2{\mathsf{T}}}},\quad\text{for}\quad t/4M^{2}\leq x\leq E_{t}+1/2,

and the fact that
{\rm Im}[w]\leq\pi t
from (
4.88
).
For the last integral in (
4.89
) we have

(4.91)

\displaystyle\begin{split}t^{3}\int_{-1}^{0}\frac{{\rm d}x}{((u-x)^{2}+(\pi t)^{2})^{2}}&=\int_{-1/t}^{0}\frac{{\rm d}x}{((u/t-x)^{2}+\pi^{2})^{2}}\geq\int_{\min\{0,u/t\}-1/(2{\mathsf{T}})}^{\min\{0,u/t\}}\frac{{\rm d}x}{((u/t-x)^{2}+\pi^{2})^{2}}\\
&\geq\int_{\min\{0,u/t\}-1/(2{\mathsf{T}})}^{\min\{0,u/t\}}\frac{{\rm d}x}{((1+1/2{\mathsf{T}})^{2}+\pi^{2})^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{2{\mathsf{T}}}\frac{1}{(2^{2}+\pi^{2})^{2}}\end{split}

where in the first statement we changed variables
x\rightarrow tx
; in the second statement we restricted the integral and used
\min\{0,u/t\}-1/(2{\mathsf{T}})\geq-1/(2t)-1/(2{\mathsf{T}})\geq-1/t
(where
u\geq-1/2
is from (
4.88
)); in the third statement we used

u\leq\xi(t)\leq t
(from (
4.88
)), so
|u/t-x|\leq 1+1/(2{\mathsf{T}})
;
and in the last inequality we used
{\mathsf{T}}\geq 1
.

To bound the numerator in (
4.87
),
we first notice that

(4.92)

\displaystyle\begin{split}x&=|(\xi(t)-tm_{0}(\xi(t)))-(w-tm_{0}(w))|\leq|\xi(t)-w|\left(1+t\int_{-1}^{0}\frac{{\rm d}x}{|w-x||\xi(t)-x|}\right)\\
&\leq|\xi(t)-w|\left(1+t\sqrt{\int_{-1}^{0}\frac{{\rm d}x}{|w-x|^{2}}\int_{-1}^{0}\frac{{\rm d}x}{|\xi(t)-x|^{2}}}\right)=2|\xi(t)-w|,\end{split}

where in the last equality we used (
4.63
). Recall from(
4.90
) that
{\rm Im}[w]\geq t/(2M\sqrt{2{\mathsf{T}}})

and from (
4.64
) that
|\xi(t)|\leq t
. Thus,

(4.93)

\displaystyle\left(1+4M\sqrt{\mathsf{T}}\right)|w|\geq|w|+|\xi(t)|\geq|w-\xi(t)|\geq\frac{x}{2},

where the last inequality is from (
4.92
).
By using the formula for
m_{0}^{\prime}(z)
from (
4.60
), with (
4.93
), we get

(4.94)

\displaystyle\big|{\rm Im}[m_{0}^{\prime}(w)]\big|=\Bigg|{\rm Im}\left[\frac{1}{w}-\frac{1}{(w+1)}\right]\Bigg|=\frac{\big|{\rm Im}[w(w+1)]\big|}{|w(w+1)|^{2}}\leq 4\frac{\big|2{\rm Re}[w]+1\big|{\rm Im}[w]}{|w|^{2}}\leq\frac{32\pi({\mathsf{T}}+1)}{(1+4M\sqrt{\mathsf{T}})^{2}}\frac{t}{x^{2}},

where we used that
|w+1|\geq 1/2
(following from (
4.88
)) in the third statement and
|{\rm Re}[w]|\leq{\mathsf{T}}
(following from (
4.88
) and
\xi(t)\leq t\leq{\mathsf{T}}
from (
4.64
)) and
{\rm Im}[w]\leq\pi t
(from (
4.88
)) in the fourth. By plugging (
4.89
), (
4.91
) and (
4.94
) into (
4.87
), we conclude that there exists a constant
C_{0}=C_{0}(\mathsf{T})>1
such that, for
|\Delta|\geq t/M
, we have

(4.95)

\displaystyle|\varrho_{t}^{\prime}(E_{t}-x)|\leq C_{0}\frac{t}{x^{2}}.

The estimates (
4.86
) and (
4.95
) together give

\displaystyle\varrho^{\prime}_{t}(E_{t}-x)\asymp\min\left\{\sqrt{\frac{1}{xt}},\frac{t}{x^{2}}\right\},\qquad\text{whenever $0\leq x\leq E_{t}+1/2$.}

This finishes the proof of (
4.87
).
∎

The following proposition bounds the fluctuations for the largest (and smallest) particle under Dyson Brownian motion run under initial data prescribed by (the classical locations of)
\mu_{0}=\textbf{1}_{[-1,0]}
; it is a quick consequence of
2.12
and
4.5
. As alluded to in the beginning of this section, observe that it provides a concentration bound with error much smaller than
n^{-2/3}
, if
t\ll n^{-2/3}
.

Proposition 4.6
.

For any real number
{\mathsf{T}}\geq 1
, there exists a constant
C=C(\mathsf{T})>1
such that the following holds. Let
\lambda_{1}(t)>\lambda_{2}(t)>\cdots>\lambda_{n}(t)
denote Dyson Brownian motion (
1.2
), with initial data given by setting
\lambda_{i}(0)=(1-2i)/(2n)
for each
1\leq i\leq n
. Denote the classical locations of
\mu_{t}
(recall
Definition

2.8
) by
\gamma_{j}(t)=\gamma_{j;n}^{\mu_{t}}
. Then for
n
large enough, we have for any
0\leq t\leq{\mathsf{T}}
that

(4.96)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}|\lambda_{1}(s)-\gamma_{1}(s)|\leq C\left(t+\frac{(\log n)\sqrt{t}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\right)\geq 1-Ce^{(-\log n)^{2}},

(4.97)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{0\leq s\leq t,\atop 1\leq i\leq n}\lambda_{i}(s)-\lambda_{1}(0)+\frac{i-1}{n}\leq C\left(t+t|\log(t)|+\frac{(\log n)\sqrt{t}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\right)\geq 1-Ce^{(-\log n)^{2}}.

Proof of
4.6
.

Let us first verify the assumptions in
2.12
with the
c
there given by
1/4{\mathfrak{C}}
here, where
{\mathfrak{C}}>1
is the constant
C(\mathsf{T})
from
4.5
. The first assumption in that proposition is verified since
\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\varrho_{t}(x)\leq 1\leq(2c)^{-1}
, where the first bound follows from (
4.52
) (and the second from the bound
c<1
, as
\mathfrak{C}>1
). To verify the second assumption, observe by
4.5
, that for each
0\leq t\leq{\mathsf{T}}
and
x\in[E_{t}^{-},E_{t}^{+}]
we have

(4.98)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\varrho^{\prime}_{t}(x)|&\leq{\mathfrak{C}}\min\left\{\sqrt{\frac{1}{t\min\{|E_{t}^{+}-x|,|E_{t}^{-}-x|\}}},\frac{t}{\min\{|E_{t}^{+}-x|,|E_{t}^{-}-x|\}^{2}}\right\}\leq\frac{{\mathfrak{C}}}{\min\{|E_{t}^{+}-x|,|E_{t}^{-}-x|\}},\end{split}

where the second inequality follows from the fact that
\min\{\sqrt{(ab)^{-1}},ab^{-2}\big\}\leq b^{-1}
for any real numbers
a,b>0
(applied with
(a,b)=\big(t,\min\{|E_{t}^{+}-x|,|E_{t}^{-}-x|\}\big)
). Furthermore, letting
\Gamma_{t}:[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
denote the inverted cummulative density function associated with
\mu_{t}
(recall (
2.13
)), we also have
\Gamma_{t}(y)-\Gamma_{t}(y^{\prime})\geq y^{\prime}-y
for any
0\leq y\leq y^{\prime}\leq 1
, since
\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\varrho_{t}(x)\leq 1
. Hence,
E_{t}^{+}=\Gamma_{t}(0)\geq\Gamma_{t}(1/2n)+1/2n=\gamma_{1}(t)+1/2n
and similarly
E_{t}^{-}\leq\gamma_{n}(t)-1/2n
. Thus, whenever
\dist(x,\{\gamma_{1}(t),\gamma_{2}(t),\cdots,\gamma_{n}(t)\})\leq c/2n
, we must have that
\dist(x,\{E_{t}^{+},E_{t}^{-}\})\geq 1/2n-c/2n\geq 1/4n
. Together with (
4.98
), this yields
|\varrho^{\prime}_{t}(x)|\leq 4{\mathfrak{C}}n=c^{-1}n
, thereby verifying the second assumption in
2.12
.

Hence, that proposition applies, and so there exists a constant
C=C(\mathsf{T})>1
such that for
n
large enough and
0\leq t\leq{\mathsf{T}}
the following holds.
For each

(4.99)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}|\lambda_{1}(s)-\gamma_{1}(s)|\leq C\left(t+\frac{(\log n)\sqrt{t}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\right)\geq 1-\OO\left(e^{-(\log n)^{2}}\right).

This gives the first statement in (
4.96
). Moreover, there exists a constant
\widetilde{C}=\widetilde{C}(\mathsf{T})>1
such that, with probability
1-\mathcal{O}(e^{-(\log n)^{2}})
we have for each
0\leq t\leq\mathsf{T}
that

\displaystyle\phantom{{}={}}\lambda_{i}(s)-\lambda_{1}(0)\leq\gamma_{i}(s)-\gamma_{1}(0)+C\left(t+\frac{(\log n)\sqrt{t}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)

\displaystyle=E_{s}^{+}+\gamma_{i}(s)-\gamma_{1}(s)-(E_{s}^{+}-\gamma_{1}(s))-\gamma_{1}(0)+C\left(t+\frac{(\log n)\sqrt{t}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq E_{s}^{+}-\frac{i-1}{n}-\frac{1}{2n}+\frac{1}{2n}+C\left(t+\frac{(\log n)\sqrt{t}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)=E_{s}^{+}+C\left(t+\frac{(\log n)\sqrt{t}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)-\frac{i-1}{n}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{C}\left(t+t|\log t|+\frac{(\log n)\sqrt{t}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)-\frac{i-1}{n}

where in the first statement we used the facts that
\gamma_{1}(0)=\lambda_{1}(0)
and
2.12
; in the third statement we used the facts that
\gamma_{1}(0)=-1/(2n)
, that (since by
4.5
we have
\varrho_{s}(y)\leq 1
)
\gamma_{i}(s)-\gamma_{1}(s)\leq-(i-1)/n
, and that
E_{s}^{+}-\gamma_{1}(s)\geq 1/(2n)
; in the fifth statement we used the upper bound of
E_{s}^{+}\leq C^{\prime}(s+s|\log s|)\leq C^{\prime}(t+t|\log t|)
from
4.5
. This establishes the second statement in (
4.99
).
∎

As a consequence of
4.6
, we next deduce the following result estimating the trajectories of the first (and last) particles under the rescaled Dyson Brownian motion (recall (
4.2
)), whose initial data is given by a uniform distribution (of density governed by the parameter
B
below) added to a delta mass (of weight governed by the parameter
M
below). It will be used in the forthcoming work
[
3
]
.

Corollary 4.7
.

For any real numbers
B,D>1
, there exist constants
C_{1}=C_{1}(B)>1
and
C_{2}=C_{2}(B,D)>1
such that the following holds. Let
n\geq k\geq 2
be integers, and let
L\in[1,k^{D}]
be a real number such that
n=L^{3/2}k
. Let
\bm{x}(t)=\big(x_{1}(t),x_{2}(t),\ldots,x_{n}(s)\big)\in\overline{\mathbb{W}}_{n}
denote rescaled Dyson Brownian motion (from (
4.2
)) with initial data
\bm{x}(0)
, run for time
t
. Suppose that, for some real number
M\geq 1
, we have

(4.100)

\displaystyle x_{i}(0)-x_{j}(0)\geq\left(\frac{j-i}{BL^{3/4}k}-M\right)k^{2/3},\qquad\text{for each $1\leq i\leq j\leq n$}.

Then for any real number
0\leq t\leq 1
, we have

(4.101)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathbb{P}\Bigg[\Big\{x_{1}(tk^{1/3})-x_{1}(0)\leq C_{1}k^{2/3}\big(tL^{3/4}(1+|\log t|^{2})+(&Mt)^{1/2}L^{3/8}+(tk^{-1})^{1/2}\log n\big)\Big\}\Bigg]\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\geq 1-C_{2}e^{-(\log n)^{2}}.\end{aligned}

Similarly, for any real number
0\leq t\leq 1
, we have

(4.102)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathbb{P}\Bigg[\Big\{x_{n}(tk^{1/3})-x_{n}(0)\geq-C_{1}k^{2/3}\big(tL^{3/4}(1+|\log t|^{2})+(&Mt)^{1/2}L^{3/8}+(tk^{-1})^{1/2}\log n\big)\Big\}\Bigg]\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\geq 1-C_{2}e^{-(\log n)^{2}}.\end{aligned}

Proof of
4.7
.

Observe that (
4.102
) follows from (
4.101
) by symmetry (that is, by negating each of the
x_{j}
, which leaves the assumption (
4.100
) satisfied). Thus, it suffices to verify (
4.101
).

To that end, we first rescale the
x_{j}
to make them satisfy the standard Dyson Brownian motion equations (
1.2
). More specifically, we rescale time by
k^{1/3}/B^{2}
and space by
n^{2/3}/(BL^{1/4})
, defining

(4.103)

\displaystyle\lambda_{i}(t)=\frac{BL^{1/4}}{n^{2/3}}x_{i}(B^{-2}k^{1/3}t).

Since
n^{-1}=(BL^{1/4}n^{-2/3})^{2}\cdot B^{-2}k^{1/3}
(as
n=L^{3/2}k
), it is quickly verified that
\{\lambda_{i}(t)\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
satisfies (
1.2
). Moreover, (since
BL^{1/4}n^{-2/3}\cdot(BL^{3/4}k^{1/3})^{-1}=n^{-1}
and
BL^{1/4}n^{-2/3}k^{2/3}=BL^{-3/4}
, both as
n=L^{3/2}k
) the initial data satisfies

(4.104)

\displaystyle\lambda_{i}(0)-\lambda_{j}(0)\geq\frac{j-i}{n}-\frac{BM}{L^{3/4}},\qquad\text{for each $1\leq i\leq j\leq n$}.

Now set
\delta=BML^{-3/4}
. Since
BL^{1/4}n^{-2/3}\cdot k^{-2/3}=BL^{-3/4}
, the claim (
4.101
) is equivalent to show that for any fixed
t\leq B^{2}
, we have with probability
1-\OO(e^{-(\log n)^{2}})
that

(4.105)

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}(t)\leq\lambda_{1}(0)+C_{1}^{\prime}(t+t|\log t|^{2}+\sqrt{\delta t}+(\log n)\sqrt{t/n}),

for some constant
C_{1}^{\prime}=C_{1}^{\prime}(B)>1
. By translating the
\lambda_{i}
if necessary, we may assume that
\lambda_{1}(0)=-(2n)^{-1}
. Moreover, by the comparison result given by the first statement in
2.10
, we may assume that the remaining
\lambda_{i}
are as large as possible so that (
4.104
) is satisfied. More specifically, denoting
m=\lceil\delta n\rceil
, we may suppose

(4.106)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\lambda_{1}(0)=\lambda_{2}(0)=\cdots=\lambda_{m}(0)=-(2n)^{-1}=\lambda_{m+1}(0)=-(2n)^{-1},\\
&\lambda_{m+i}(0)-\lambda_{m+i+1}(0)=n^{-1},\quad\text{for each $1\leq i\leq n-m+1$},\end{aligned}

where the last constraint can be omitted if
m\geq n
.

For the special case
m=0
, (
4.105
) follows from
4.6
, which in fact implies the slightly stronger estimate: there exists a constant
C_{0}=C_{0}(\mathsf{T})>1
such that, with probability
1-\OO(e^{-(\log n)^{2}})
, we have for each
0\leq t\leq\mathsf{T}
that

(4.107)

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}(t)\leq\lambda_{1}(0)+C_{0}(t+t|\log t|+(\log n)\sqrt{t/n}).

Next assume that
m\geq 1
. We consider two cases, depending on if
\sqrt{mt/n}\leq t|\log t|
or
\sqrt{mt/n}\geq t|\log t|
.

First assume that
\sqrt{mt/n}\leq t|\log t|
, so that
m/n\leq t|\log t|^{2}
.
We consider another Dyson Brownian motion with initial data given by

\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}(0)=m/n-(i-1/2)/n
for
1\leq i\leq n
.
The new Dyson Brownian motion satisfies the assumptions in
4.6
(up to translation by
m/n
). Thus that proposition yields, with probability
1-\OO(e^{-(\log n)^{2}})
, that

(4.108)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widetilde{\lambda}_{1}(t)+1/(2n)&\leq(m/n)+C_{0}(t+t|\log t|+\log n\sqrt{t/n})\\
&\leq C_{0}(t+t|\log t|+t|\log t|^{2}+\log n\sqrt{t/n})\leq 2C_{0}(t+t|\log t|^{2}+\log n\sqrt{t/n})\end{split}

Moreover, by construction (and (
4.106
)), the new Dyson Brownian motion satisfies
\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}(0)\geq\lambda_{i}(0)
for
1\leq i\leq n
. By the first statement in
2.10
, we can couple the
\lambda_{j}
with the
\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}
such that
\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}(t)\geq\lambda_{i}(t)
for
1\leq i\leq n
. The claim (
4.105
) then follows (
4.108
) (with the fact that
\lambda_{1}(0)=-(2n)^{-1}
).

Now assume instead that
\sqrt{mt/n}\geq t|\log t|
, so that
m/n\geq t|\log t|^{2}
. We consider another Dyson Brownian motion with initial data given by

\displaystyle\widehat{\lambda}_{1}(0)=\widehat{\lambda}_{2}(0)=\cdots=\widehat{\lambda}_{m+1}(0)=r,\quad\text{and}\quad\widehat{\lambda}_{i}(0)=\lambda_{i}(0),\quad\text{for $m+2\leq i\leq n$},

where

(4.109)

\displaystyle r=3C_{2}(t+\sqrt{(m+1)t/n}+\log n\sqrt{t/n})-(1/2n).

and
C_{2}
is the maximum of the two constants
C(\mathsf{T})
from
2.13
and
4.6
. Then the first statement in
2.10
yields a coupling between the
\lambda_{j}
and
\widehat{\lambda}_{j}
such that
\lambda_{1}(t)\leq\widehat{\lambda}_{1}(t)
. Hence, to verify (
4.105
) (with the fact that
\lambda_{1}(0)=(-2n)^{-1}
), it suffices to show with probability at least
1-\OO(e^{-(\log n)^{2}})
that

(4.110)

\displaystyle\widehat{\lambda}_{1}(t)+(2n)^{-1}\leq C_{1}^{\prime}(t+t|\log t|^{2}+\sqrt{\delta t}+(\log n)\sqrt{t/n}).

To verify (
4.110
), we will first show that there is likely a gap between
\widehat{\lambda}_{m+1}(s)
and
\widehat{\lambda}_{m+2}(s)
for
s\in[0,t]
, which will require both an upper on the
\widehat{\lambda}_{i}(s)
for
m+2\leq i\leq n
and a lower bound on the
\widehat{\lambda}_{i}(s)
for
1\leq i\leq m+1
. We begin with the upper bound, which will proceed by comparing
\widehat{\lambda}_{i}(s)
to Dyson Brownian motion
\widehat{y}_{i}(s)
with initial data
\widehat{y}_{i}(0)=-(2i+1)(2n)^{-1}
for each
1\leq i\leq n
; we claim that it is possible to couple
\widehat{\lambda}_{i}
with
\widehat{y}_{i}
such that
\widehat{\lambda}_{i}(s)\leq\widehat{y}_{i-m-1}(s)
for each
s\in[0,t]
and
m+2\leq i\leq n
.

To show this, we first compare
\{\widehat{\lambda}_{i}(s)\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
with Dyson Brownian motion with initial data
y_{1}(0)=y_{2}(0)=\cdots=y_{m+1}(0)=\infty
, and
y_{i}(0)=\lambda_{i}(0)=(2m-2i+1)(2n)^{-1}
for
m+2\leq i\leq n
. Then,
y_{i}(0)\geq\widehat{\lambda}_{i}(0)
for each
1\leq i\leq n
, and so the first part of
2.10
yields a coupling between the
\widehat{\lambda}_{i}
and
y_{i}
such that, with probability
1-\OO(e^{-(\log n)^{2}})
, we have for each
s\in[0,t]
and
1\leq i\leq n
that
\widehat{\lambda}_{i}(s)\leq y_{i}(s)
. Let
\breve{y}_{i}(s)
denote Dyson Brownian motion with initial data
\breve{y}_{i}(0)=y_{i+m+1}(0)=-(2i+1)(2n)^{-1}
for
1\leq i\leq n-m-1
and
\breve{y}_{i}(0)=-\infty
for
n-m\leq i\leq n
. Then, the above coupling between
\widehat{\lambda}
and
\widehat{y}
yields
\widehat{\lambda}_{i}(s)\leq\breve{y}_{i-m-1}(s)
for each
m+2\leq i\leq n
. Since
\breve{y}_{i}(0)\leq\widehat{y}_{i}(0)
for each
1\leq i\leq n
, the first part of
2.10
yields a coupling between
\breve{y}_{i}(s)
and
\widehat{y}_{i}(s)
such that
\breve{y}_{i}(s)\leq\widehat{y}_{i}(s)
for each
s\in[0,t]
and
1\leq i\leq n
. Combining these two couplings confirms the existence of one between
\widehat{\lambda}_{i}(s)
and
\widehat{y}_{i}(s)
such that
\widehat{\lambda}_{i}(s)\leq\widehat{y}_{i-m-1}(s)
for each
s\in[0,t]
and
m+2\leq i\leq n
. Hence, with probability
1-\OO(e^{-(\log n)^{2}})
, we have for each
s\in[0,t]
and
m+2\leq i\leq n
that

(4.111)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \widehat{\lambda}_{i}(s)\leq\widehat{y}_{i-m-1}(s)&\leq-1/(2n)+C_{2}(t+t|\log t|+\log n\sqrt{t/n})-(i-m-1)/n,\end{aligned}

where the last inequality is from
4.6
(applied with the
\lambda_{i}
there given by
\widehat{y}_{i}
here, translated by
mn^{-1}
) and the fact that
\widehat{y}_{1}(0)=-3(2n)^{-1}
.

To lower bound the
\widehat{\lambda}_{i}(s)
, we next compare
\{\widehat{\lambda}_{i}(s)\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
with Dyson Brownian motion starting from
z_{1}(0)=z_{2}(0)=\cdots=z_{m+1}(0)=r
, and
z_{i}(0)=-\infty
for
i\geq m+2
. Since
z_{i}(0)\leq\widehat{\lambda}_{i}(0)
for each
1\leq i\leq n
, the first part of
2.10
yields a coupling between the
z_{i}
and
\widehat{\lambda}_{i}
such that, with probability
1-\OO(e^{-(\log n)^{2}})
, we have for each
s\in[0,t]
and
1\leq i\leq m+1
that

(4.112)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widehat{\lambda}_{i}(s)\geq z_{i}(s)&\geq r-C_{2}(\sqrt{(m+1)t/n}+\log n\sqrt{t/n})-1/(2n)\\
&\geq 2C_{2}(t+\sqrt{(m+1)t/n}+\log n\sqrt{t/n})-1/(2n)\geq 2C_{2}(t+t|\log t|+\log n\sqrt{t/n})-(1/2n),\end{split}

where the second inequality is from
2.13
(translated by
r
); the third inequality uses that
r=3C_{2}(t+\sqrt{(m+1)t/n}+\log n\sqrt{t/n})
; and the last inequality uses that
m/n\geq t|\log t|^{2}
.

Comparing (
4.111
) and (
4.112
), we deduce that with probability at least
1-\OO(e^{-(\log n)^{2}})
that, for each
s\in[0,t]
and
1\leq i\leq m+1<m+2\leq j\leq n
,

(4.113)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widehat{\lambda}_{i}(s)-\widehat{\lambda}_{j}(s)&\geq C_{2}(t+t|\log t|+\log n\sqrt{t/n})+\frac{j-m-1}{n}.\end{split}

In what follows, we restrict to the event
\mathscr{E}
on which the bound (
4.113
) holds, observing that
\mathbb{P}[\mathscr{E}]\geq 1-\OO(e^{-(\log n)^{2}})
.

On the event
\mathscr{E}
, the stochastic differential equation (
1.2
) implies, for any
s\in[0,t]
and
1\leq i\leq m+1
, that

(4.114)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\rm d}\widehat{\lambda}_{i}(s)&\leq\left(\frac{2}{\beta n}\right)^{1/2}{\rm d}B_{i}(s)+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j:j\neq i,\atop j\leq m+1}\frac{1}{\widehat{\lambda}_{i}(s)-\widehat{\lambda}_{j}(s)}+\displaystyle\sum_{m+2}^{n}\frac{1}{C_{2}(t+t|\log t|)n+j-m-1}\\
&\leq\left(\frac{2}{\beta n}\right)^{1/2}{\rm d}B_{i}(s)+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j:j\neq i,\atop j\leq m+1}\frac{1}{\widehat{\lambda}_{i}(s)-\widehat{\lambda}_{j}(s)}+\int_{C_{2}(t+t|\log t|)}^{C_{2}(t+t|\log t|)+1}\frac{{\rm d}x}{x}\\
&\leq\left(\frac{2}{\beta n}\right)^{1/2}{\rm d}B_{i}(s)+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j:j\neq i,\atop j\leq m+1}\frac{1}{\widehat{\lambda}_{i}(s)-\widehat{\lambda}_{j}(s)}+\log C_{2}^{\prime}+C_{2}^{\prime}|\log t|,\end{split}

for some constant
C_{2}^{\prime}=C_{2}^{\prime}(\mathsf{T})>1
. Here, the first line follows from (
1.2
) and (
4.113
); the second from the fact that
x^{-1}
is decreasing; and the third from performing the integration and using the facts that
\log\big(C_{2}(t+t|\log t|)+1\big)\leq\log C_{2}+\log\big(t+t|\log t|+1\big)\leq\log C_{2}+\mathsf{T}+\mathsf{T}|\log t|
and
\big|\log\big(C_{2}(t+t|\log t|)\big)\big|\leq\log C_{2}+|\log t|+\log\big(1+|\log t|\big)\leq\log C_{2}+2|\log t|
.

(
4.114
) is the same stochastic differential equation as the
(m+1)
-particle Dyson Brownian motion starting from a delta mass at
r
with an extra drift
\log C_{2}+2C_{2}|\log t|
. Specifically, letting
w_{i}(s)
denote Dyson Brownian motion (
1.2
) with initial data
w_{i}(0)=r
for
1\leq i\leq m+1
, we find from (
4.114
) that on
\mathscr{E}
we have
\widehat{\lambda}_{i}(s)\leq w_{i}(s)+\big(\log C_{2}^{\prime}+C_{2}^{\prime}|\log t|\big)s
, for each
s\in[0,t]
and
1\leq i\leq m+1
. Therefore, since
\mathbb{P}[\mathscr{E}]\geq 1-\OO(e^{-(\log n)^{2}})
,
2.13
and a union bound yields that, with high probability
1-\OO(e^{-(\log n)^{2}})
, we have for some constant
C_{3}=C_{3}(\mathsf{T})>1
that

\displaystyle\widehat{\lambda}_{1}(t)\leq w_{1}(t)+\big(\log C_{2}^{\prime}+C_{2}^{\prime}|\log t|\big)t

\displaystyle\leq r+C_{2}\left(\sqrt{(m+1)t/n}+\frac{(\log n)\sqrt{t}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)+(\log C_{2}^{\prime}+2C_{2}|\log t|)t

\displaystyle\leq C_{3}(t+\sqrt{(m+1)t/n}+t|\log t|+\log n\sqrt{t/n})-(1/2n)

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{3}(t+t|\log t|^{2}+\sqrt{\delta t+2t/n}+(\log n)\sqrt{t/n})-(1/2n)

\displaystyle\leq 6C_{3}(t+t|\log t|^{2}+\sqrt{\delta t}+(\log n)\sqrt{t/n})-(1/2n).

where the third bound follows from (
4.109
); the fourth from the facts that
\delta n\geq\lceil\delta n\rceil-1\geq m-1
and that
t+t|\log t|^{2}\geq|\log t|
; and the fifth from the facts that
\sqrt{\delta t+2tn^{-1}}\leq\sqrt{\delta t}+\sqrt{2}\cdot\sqrt{tn^{-1}}
and that
2^{1/2}\leq 3\log 2\leq 3\log n
. This implies (
4.110
) and thus the corollary.
∎
